# CareDroid Quality Scorecard

**CareDroid — Emergency Department operations platform** (Vite + React frontend, NestJS backend, embedded clinical copilot). This file tracks a long-running, cycle-by-cycle quality-improvement program: one well-scoped, evidence-backed lever per cycle, never a fabricated score.

**Last updated: 2026-07-31 (Cycle 248 — continued down the "unvalidated `@Body()`" baseline right after Cycle 247, per the user's "let's do the next cycle." Picked the next-largest remaining cluster: `ClinicalIntelligenceController`'s 13 routes, all pure `PlatformSystemsService.demo()` passthroughs with zero real persistence or field-specific logic of their own (confirmed by direct read — `demo()` never inspects payload contents, just echoes it into a synthetic contract response). A frontend sweep found the same "no real caller" pattern Cycle 247 found for governance: 10 of the 13 routes are only ever reached via `PlatformSystemPage.tsx`'s one generic "Run demo contract" button, which always sends an identical fixed shape (`{capabilityId, patientId, mode, source, confirmationRequired}`) regardless of capability; the other 2 (`approveDocument`, `exportDocument`) have no caller of any kind, not even the demo button — confirmed by checking `src/data/platformSystems.tsx`'s capability registry has no entry pointing at either route at all. With no live payload to preserve and no service-side field reads to mirror, the 13 new DTOs (`backend/src/modules/platform-systems/dto/clinical-intelligence-actions.dto.ts`, built on a shared `PlatformDemoContractFieldsDto` base covering the one real caller's actual shape) formalize each route's intended request shape using the field names already encoded in this controller's own pre-existing unit-test fixtures (`chiefComplaint`, `goal`, `question`, `decision`, `runId`, `eventType`, `transcript`, `audioRef`, `specialty`, `medication`, `approvedBy`, `format`) — documenting what each AI capability is *for*, not a reverse-engineered live contract. Added a live-`ValidationPipe` proof test (`backend-contract-hardening.spec.ts`) matching Cycles 241/247's precedent. Live-boot-verified against the real compiled server (not just unit tests): a PHYSICIAN JWT hitting `POST /api/clinical-intelligence/calculator-recommender/suggest` got a real `200` for both the demo-button shape and the intended `chiefComplaint`/`symptoms` shape, a real `400` (`"property sqlInjectAttempt should not exist"`) for an injected field, and a real `401` with no token. Updated `test/body-validation-coverage.spec.ts`'s baseline 78 → 65. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both 0 errors. Full backend suite: **271/271 suites, 2285/2285 tests, zero regressions** (+1 test vs. Cycle 247's 2284 — the new live-ValidationPipe proof). Production build (`nest build`) verified clean. See the updated roadmap row below.**

**Last updated: 2026-07-30 (Cycle 245 — Cycle C of the "Mission Intelligence Engine v1" plan: wrote real `SurgeCapacityService` test coverage (11 cases across all 5 public methods, previously zero coverage existed), and hit a genuine, well-evidenced environment wall while trying to run it. Added `mongodb-memory-server` as a devDependency (confirmed zero new vulnerable chains via `npm audit`, unrelated to the pre-existing tracked `brace-expansion` chain, item #21). It downloaded a real `mongod` binary successfully (`~/.cache/mongodb-binaries/mongod-x64-win32-8.2.6.exe`) but then failed with `spawn UNKNOWN`. Didn't assume this was a code bug — confirmed it's this sandbox's Application Control policy (the **same class of restriction already documented for this program's `esbuild`/Vitest blocker**, SCORECARD item #3, re-verified Cycle 166) via 3 independent checks: a bare Node `child_process.spawn()` of the exact binary, entirely outside `mongodb-memory-server`'s own code, fails identically; invoking it directly from PowerShell surfaces the policy's own explicit message, *"An Application Control policy has blocked this file"*; Docker (a possible alternate path to a real MongoDB) isn't installed here either. Given this is a hard local block with no available workaround, the test suite is **written and type-checked but not executed/verified in this sandbox** — said plainly, not glossed over, matching this program's core discipline.

Because the environment can't run it, did the next best thing available: a careful line-by-line manual review against the real `SurgeCapacityService` implementation in place of empirical execution — and this caught a real bug I would otherwise have shipped unverified: the "surgeons at exactly 80% utilization" test used `surgeonsAvailable:1, surgeonsTotal:5` (utilization = exactly 0.8), but the service's threshold check is `> 0.8` (strictly greater) — the test would have failed if it could run. Fixed to `surgeonsTotal:6` (utilization ≈0.833, unambiguously above the boundary). A second, equally important problem this cycle solved: a plain `.spec.ts` file matching the main Jest config's `testRegex` would have silently broken **every future cycle's** "full suite green" verification the moment this sandbox tried to run it via a normal `npm test`. Renamed the file to `surge-capacity.service.mongo-spec.ts` and added a dedicated `test/jest-mongo.json` config + `npm run test:mongo` script — the exact same isolation idiom this repo already uses for `.e2e-spec.ts` files, applied to a new category (Mongo-dependent tests) for the first time. Verified the isolation actually works: `npx jest --listTests` (main config) does **not** discover the new file; `npx jest --config test/jest-mongo.json --listTests` does. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both 0 errors (this file included — TypeScript still checks it even though Jest won't run it by default). Full backend suite unaffected: **271/271 suites, 2283/2283 tests, zero regressions** (identical to Cycle 244 — confirms the new file is correctly excluded, not silently missing from the count). Production build (`nest build`) also verified clean. This test suite should be run via `npm run test:mongo` in a real CI/dev environment without this sandbox's restriction before it can be honestly marked "passing." See roadmap row below.**

**Last updated: 2026-07-30 (Cycle 244 — Cycle B of the "Mission Intelligence Engine v1" plan (`fancy-swinging-ladybug.md`): closed Sentinel's 6 remaining unvalidated `@Body()` gaps and proved CAD/AVL webhook ingestion end-to-end with a real e2e test. First attempt used a per-route `ValidationPipe({whitelist:false})` override on `@Body()` for the 2 genuinely "vendor-agnostic" routes (`ingestCad`, `upsertInbound`) — a real e2e test (not a mocked unit test, which would never have caught this) proved this doesn't work: Nest chains the global `ValidationPipe` (`forbidNonWhitelisted:true`, set in `main.ts`) with any param-level pipe rather than replacing it, so the global one already rejects unknown fields with a 400 before a param-level override ever runs. Rather than ship a broken fix, traced what "vendor-agnostic" actually means by reading both real consumers of these bodies: `normalizeWebhookPayload()` (`adapters/cad-avl.adapter.ts`) and `KNOWN_ALIASES` in `lib/sentinel/nemsisMap.ts` — the "flexibility" is a **bounded, finite alias table** (33 known alternate field spellings mapping to 13 canonical NEMSIS fields), not truly open-ended; any field outside that table was already being silently discarded by these functions before this cycle, so real whitelist enforcement changes nothing functionally for a legitimate caller. Enumerated the full alias set into a shared `NemsisClinicalFieldsDto` base class (avoiding ~40 lines of duplication between `IngestCadDto`/`UpsertInboundDto`), removed the ineffective pipe override, and got genuine, working whitelist protection with zero regression risk. `PrepRecommendationDto`/`RaiseAlarmDto`/`AlarmActionDto`/`ReviewAiDto` were simpler, fully-enumerable bodies with no such tension. While rebuilding `raiseAlarm`, found `SentinelAlarmService.raise()`'s real input type (`RaiseAlarmInput`) already supports 3 optional fields (`organizationId`, `metadata`, `actorId`) the controller never wired through — added them to the DTO and the service call, closing a small, adjacent, real gap found incidentally.

New `test/sentinel-webhook-ingest.e2e-spec.ts`: a real NestJS app, a real (minimal, self-contained JWT-only) auth module, real SQLite persistence, and real HTTP requests via `supertest` — proving a vendor-shaped CAD/AVL webhook payload flows through ingestion → the real Haversine ETA engine → the real geofence engine → real `sentinel_units`/`sentinel_positions`/`sentinel_eta`/`sentinel_outbox`/`sentinel_geofence_events`/`sentinel_alarms` rows, plus idempotency (replayed `eventId` doesn't duplicate) and a malformed-payload path. Building this test surfaced 2 further real findings, both documented rather than silently worked around: (1) the codebase's own established e2e pattern (`AuthModule` + a handful of entities, used by `test/two-factor.e2e-spec.ts`) is **already broken today** — `PlatformAssetsModule`'s `TenantIsolationGuard` needs `TenantContextService`, not available outside the full app graph, confirmed by running that pre-existing spec and getting the identical `EntityMetadataNotFoundError`/DI-resolution failure independent of this cycle's changes; this test deliberately used a minimal, self-contained JWT module instead of chasing that fix, which is out of scope here. (2) `SentinelTrackingService.ingestCadEvents()` (the webhook path) has no `config.enabled` guard at all — only `pollAdapters()` (the cron/mock-adapter path) checks `SENTINEL_ENABLED`; also, `onModuleInit()`'s one-time default-geofence seeding only runs when the flag was on at boot. This asymmetry means a webhook call could write real unit/position/outbox rows even with `SENTINEL_ENABLED=false`, though geofence transitions wouldn't fire meaningfully without the seeded fences. Documented, not fixed — a real design question (should the master switch gate the webhook path too?) orthogonal to this cycle's DTO-hardening scope. Updated `test/body-validation-coverage.spec.ts`'s baseline 123 → 117. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both 0 errors. Full backend suite (jest, non-e2e): **271/271 suites, 2283/2283 tests, zero regressions** — unchanged from Cycle 243, since this cycle modified existing unit tests rather than adding new ones at that tier; the new e2e suite (4/4 passing) runs under the separate `test:e2e` config, matching how every other `*.e2e-spec.ts` file in this repo is isolated from the main suite. Production build (`nest build`) also verified clean. See roadmap row below (updates Cycle 243's).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-30 (Cycle 243 — first cycle of a new, explicitly-scoped-down program: the user asked for a sweeping "Medical Intelligence Platform" vision (IoT/robotics/drones, a Mission Intelligence Engine, digital twins, a hybrid edge/cloud AI fabric); 3 parallel research passes over the actual codebase found almost none of it exists — most matching modules (`telemetry`, `fleet`, `surveillance`, `hospital-map`, several "digital twin" services) are honest, self-labeled demo/fixture data, and zero MQTT/ROS2/DDS/BLE/LoRaWAN/OPC UA/DICOM code exists anywhere. Two real exceptions stood out, fully built but disabled by default: **Sentinel** (`backend/src/modules/sentinel/`, a real DB-backed EMS/AVL command engine) and **SurgeCapacityService** (`backend/src/services/surge-capacity.service.ts`, real mass-casualty-incident/disaster-surge logic). Per the user's explicit choice (via `AskUserQuestion`, confirmed before any code was written), this program now activates and hardens what's already built into a real "Mission Intelligence Engine v1," not new greenfield IoT/robotics/AI-fabric work — a 4-cycle plan (`fancy-swinging-ladybug.md`) approved via Plan Mode with 4 explicit sign-offs (surge-activation permission scope, unification scope, route-mounting semantics, and what "verified" can honestly mean without a real MongoDB in this sandbox).

**Cycle A (this cycle) — fixed Surge's real auth/validation gap and migrated it off bare Express.** `backend/src/api/surge.routes.ts` had zero auth/validation code of its own (confirmed by direct read); when mounted (only when `ENABLE_MONGOOSE_EMERGENCY_OS=true` + `MONGODB_URI` set), it did get JWT + coarse `READ_PHI`/`WRITE_PHI` middleware (confirmed via `main.ts:62-68`'s `createLegacyApiAuthMiddleware`, itself commented "security audit D-path"), but any NURSE or PHYSICIAN (both hold `WRITE_PHI`) could already activate/deactivate a hospital-wide MCI or batch-create patients — no operation-specific permission, and zero request-body validation since raw Express bypasses Nest's `ValidationPipe` entirely. Added 3 new permissions (`VIEW_SURGE_COMMAND`, `ACTIVATE_SURGE_MODE`, `INGEST_SURGE_PATIENTS`) with role grants confirmed by the user: ADMIN gets all 3; PHYSICIAN gets view + activate (attending/incident-commander authority); NURSE gets view + ingest (bedside/triage batch intake) but not activation. Migrated `surge.routes.ts` to a real `SurgeController` (`backend/src/modules/surge/`), following `SentinelController`'s proven pattern: `@UseGuards(AuthGuard('jwt'), AuthorizationGuard)`, per-route `@RequirePermission`, and 3 real `class-validator` DTOs (`ActivateSurgeDto`, `BatchEmsIntakeDto` with nested `@ValidateNested()` vitals/patients arrays, `DeactivateSurgeDto`) — closing the Nest-`ValidationPipe`-bypass gap this program's Cycles 241-242 spent 2 cycles finding and fixing elsewhere, structurally, not just cosmetically, on a controller that never appeared in that audit because it didn't exist as a NestJS controller yet. Also found and fixed the frontend route path: the real caller (`src/services/surgeApi.ts`, confirmed live via `src/data/backendHttpRouteInventory.ts`) always used `/api/emergency/surge/*`, not the bare `/api/surge/*` the old dual-mounted Express router also exposed — the new controller uses `@Controller('emergency/surge')` as the single canonical path, matching the real, already-documented frontend contract exactly, dropping the redundant, unused alias. Made `SurgeCapacityService` `@Injectable()` (harmless — confirmed nothing else in the codebase listens to its `EventEmitter` events or depends on its current shape; the legacy plain-`new` singleton in `services/index.ts`'s health registry is untouched, mirroring the same dual-instantiation pattern already documented for `EmergencyPatientService` in `service-registry.ts`). Per the confirmed routing decision, routes are **always mounted** — the controller checks `mongoose.connection.readyState` and returns a clean `503 ServiceUnavailableException` ("requires MongoDB") instead of an unhandled 500 when the dependency isn't configured, rather than the old conditional-404-when-disabled behavior. Updated the stale `backendHttpRouteInventory.ts`/2 pre-existing test files (`app.controller.spec.ts`, `routes-registry.spec.ts`) that hardcoded the old `/api/surge`/`/surge` path. Documented `SENTINEL_ENABLED`/`SENTINEL_MOCK_ADAPTER`/`SENTINEL_FLEET_BRIDGE`/`ENABLE_MONGOOSE_EMERGENCY_OS`/`MONGODB_URI` in `backend/.env.example` (previously undocumented anywhere — confirmed by grep). New `surge.controller.spec.ts`: 13/13 passing (mocked-service behavior tests + a permission-decorator coverage block matching Cycles 238-240's established pattern). `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both 0 errors. Full backend suite: **271/271 suites, 2283/2283 tests, zero regressions** (+1 suite/+13 tests vs. Cycle 242's 270/270, 2270/2270 baseline). **Real live-boot verification, not just tests**: built and booted the compiled server, confirmed `Nest application successfully started` with no DI-resolution errors from the new module, and confirmed both `GET /api/emergency/surge/status` and `POST /api/emergency/surge/activate` return a real `401 Unauthorized` (not 404) with no JWT — proving the routes are genuinely live and guarded, not just type-checked. No MongoDB is available in this sandbox (confirmed: no service in `docker-compose.yml`/`docker-compose.app.yml`, no `MONGODB_URI` in any `.env*`), so the 503-path and `SurgeCapacityService`'s actual Mongo-backed logic remain unverified against a real database until Cycle C (`mongodb-memory-server`) — not claimed as done here. See roadmap item below.**

**Last updated: 2026-07-30 (Cycle 242 — continued working down Cycle 241's 128-entry unvalidated-`@Body()` baseline, one verified-safe batch at a time, per that cycle's own established pace. Picked 5 more of the cleanest, lowest-risk instances: `AuthController.resetPassword` was the standout — real defense-in-depth already exists in `AuthService.resetPassword()` (a strict 64-char-hex token regex, an `if (!password || password.length < 8)` check), but since Nest's `ValidationPipe` silently skips validation on the plain-object body type, a non-string `password` (e.g. a client sending a number or array) would bypass that length check entirely (`.length` on a number is `undefined`, so the comparison never throws) and crash `bcrypt.hash()` with an unhandled 500 instead of a clean 400 — the most security-relevant instance fixed this cycle, on the password-reset flow itself. Converted to a real `ResetPasswordDto` (`@IsString`/`@MinLength(8)`/`@MaxLength(128)`, matching `RegisterDto`'s existing password-strength convention). Also converted 4 small, fully-enumerable, low-stakes bodies with the same rigor: `ClinicalAlertsController.acknowledge`/`dismiss` (demo-backed clinical-alert workflow — added `@IsISO8601`/`@MaxLength` constraints), `CollaborationHubController.typing` (ephemeral typing indicator), `NotificationController.toggleAllNotifications` (self-scoped to the caller's own `req.user.id`, added alongside the module's existing `UpdatePreferencesDto` convention). Each followed the same "read every real caller, verify the field set is small and fully known before locking down validation" discipline as Cycle 241 — no risk of silently dropping a legitimate field, unlike the `EmergencyPatient`-adjacent routes that cycle deliberately left alone. Updated `test/body-validation-coverage.spec.ts`'s pinned baseline from 128 → 123 entries (verified the guard still catches drift: temporarily deleted one entry, confirmed the exact expected failure, restored it — same discipline as Cycles 237/241). `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both 0 errors. Full backend suite: **270/270 suites, 2270/2270 tests, zero regressions** (suite/test counts unchanged from Cycle 241 — this cycle extended existing spec files rather than adding new ones). 123 lower-priority instances remain, tracked in the same baseline for future cycles to continue working down. See roadmap item below (updates Cycle 241's row).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-30 (Cycle 241 — moved on from the `AuthorizationGuard` bug class (Cycles 238–240 exhausted it) to a different, adjacent one on the same backend: whether `@Body()` request payloads actually get validated by Nest's global `ValidationPipe` (`whitelist: true, forbidNonWhitelisted: true`, set in `main.ts`). They often don't — confirmed by reading NestJS's own `ValidationPipe.toValidate()`, which explicitly skips `[String, Boolean, Number, Array, Object]` metatypes and returns the raw parsed body untouched; a TypeScript `interface`/`type` alias reflects as plain `Object` at runtime (interfaces are erased from emitted JS), so any `@Body()` typed as an interface, inline object literal, `Record<string, any>`, or `any` gets **zero** whitelist/validation protection, silently, no matter how strict the global config looks. Built a `ts.Program`-based audit (full `TypeChecker`, not grep — grep can't tell a class from an interface through an import, or resolve aliased types like `SmartIntakeCreateInput`) walking every real `@Post`/`@Patch`/`@Put` `@Body()` parameter across every controller: **135 of 225 (60%) get no Nest-level validation at all**, including the primary patient-creation pathway (`SmartIntakeCreateInput` is a `type`, not a `class`) and several parameters literally named `...Dto` that are actually plain interfaces (`StartSimulationDto`, `CreateTrainingRunDto`, `CreateEvaluationRunDto`, `TelemetryIngestDto` — the naming implies protection that doesn't exist). Investigated real severity rather than treating all 135 as equally alarming: `EmergencyPatient` (the entity `PlatformSystemsController`'s unvalidated `createEmergencyPatient`/`updateEmergencyPatient` write to) has no privilege/tenant-scoping fields — Cycle 223 deliberately chose to spread the full input rather than risk silently dropping legitimate fields again, so the residual risk there is data-integrity, not privilege escalation; most `governance.controller.ts`/`platform-governance.controller.ts` routes read specific named fields off the raw body (`body.status`, `body.expiresAt`, etc.) rather than spreading it into a persisted entity, the same "safe despite no Nest validation" shape Cycle 239 already established for `createReferral`. Given the scale (135 instances, ~30 files) and the demonstrated risk of a rushed fix (an incomplete whitelist class would silently re-drop fields, the exact bug Cycle 223 fixed), did not attempt a blanket fix. Instead: **fixed the cleanest, safest, fully-verified subset this cycle** — 4 small interfaces/types genuinely named "Dto," with small, fully-enumerable, all-optional-or-simple field sets, converted to real `class-validator`-decorated classes with no risk of dropping fields (`StartSimulationDto`/`SubmitSimulationStepDto`/`CompleteSimulationDto` in `simulation.types.ts`, `CreateTrainingRunDto`/`EvaluateTrainingRunDto` in `training.types.ts`, `CreateEvaluationRunDto` in `evaluation.types.ts`, `TelemetryIngestDto` in `platform-telemetry.controller.ts` — the last on a route reachable with **no authentication at all**, the most exposed instance found). Extended the pre-existing `test/backend-contract-hardening.spec.ts` (which already spot-checked 3 unrelated DTOs for this exact pattern, just not systematically) with a live mechanism proof: instantiate Nest's real `ValidationPipe` and confirm it now rejects an injected unexpected field on the fixed DTOs — payloads that would have passed silently before this cycle. Added a new permanent regression test, `test/body-validation-coverage.spec.ts`, pinning the exact remaining 128-entry baseline (135 − 7 fixed) so future drift in *either* direction (a new unvalidated route added, or an existing one fixed without updating the baseline) fails loudly with a precise diff — verified the guard actually catches drift by temporarily deleting one baseline entry, confirming the exact expected failure, then restoring it, the same "prove the guard works" discipline Cycle 237 established for its atomicity-invariant test. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both 0 errors. Full backend suite: **270/270 suites, 2270/2270 tests, zero regressions** (+1 suite/+2 tests vs. Cycle 240's 269/269, 2268/2268 baseline). See new roadmap item below.**

**Last updated: 2026-07-30 (Cycle 240 — closed out Cycle 239's own follow-up item: the same AST audit had found 4 more controllers using `AuthorizationGuard` with undecorated routes (`CollaborationHubController` 20, `NativeAiController` 9 — corrected from Cycle 239's "8" miscount, `AuditController` 2, `UsersController` 1). Rather than assuming "undecorated = fail-open bug" the way it genuinely was for `EmergencyOsController`, read every backing service method before touching anything — and the result was mostly a **confirmed-clean audit, not a bug list**. `AuditController.syncAuditEvent`/`getMyLogs` and `UsersController.getProfile` all resolve exclusively off `req.user.id` (never a body/query-supplied id), so they're correctly self-scoped to the caller's own data — "authenticated" is the right bar, no permission needed, left untouched. `CollaborationHubController` turned out to already have a real, independently-designed, Slack/Teams-style authorization model enforced in the *service* layer — `assertAccess()` (channel membership, called at 12 real sites, traced one by one), `getOwnedMessage()` (sender-or-moderator check for edit/delete), and self-scoped membership-row lookups for read-state/preferences — verified by reading every one of the 20 flagged methods' actual service implementation, not trusting the controller's own header comment or an existing test's claim at face value (the same "verify, don't trust a comment" discipline Cycle 235 applied to a false `evaluateGate()` "blocks" claim). 19 of 20 confirmed genuinely safe as-is. The 1 real exception: `analyticsSummary` calls `getAnalyticsSummary(organizationId, windowDays)` — no `userId` parameter at all — and aggregates message/channel counts, unresolved-mention counts, and incident-resolution timings across **every** channel in the org, including patient threads and incident channels the caller never joined, with none of the membership filtering every other method has. Fixed with `VIEW_ANALYTICS`; updated the pre-existing `collaboration-hub.controller.spec.ts` (which already correctly pinned `getPatientThread` as the *one* documented exception before this cycle) to add `analyticsSummary` as a second one, rather than leaving a stale blanket assertion. `NativeAiController`'s 9 routes were a real, EmergencyOsController-shaped gap: `routePatient`/`getClinicalAcuity`/`evaluateTriage`/`inferSpecialists` all accept a real client-supplied `Patient`/`Patient[]` body and run it through `sourceState: 'live'` clinical routing/acuity/triage-inference logic (confirmed by direct read of `native-ai.service.ts`) — fixed with `READ_PHI`. `addTriageRule` mutates a **global** structured triage-rule set affecting all future evaluations platform-wide, not a per-patient record — fixed with `MANAGE_CLINICAL_POLICY` (ADMIN-only, matching its policy-level blast radius); `listTriageRules` paired with `VIEW_GOVERNANCE` (the permission `MANAGE_CLINICAL_POLICY` already implies per the hierarchy). `getRegistry`/`getDrift`/`evaluateDrift` (AI model registry + drift monitoring, no patient data) fixed with `VIEW_OBSERVABILITY`, matching Cycle 239's precedent for the same category. New regression spec `native-ai.controller-authorization.spec.ts`: 10/10 passing. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both 0 errors. Full backend suite: **269/269 suites, 2268/2268 tests, zero regressions** (+1 suite/+11 tests vs. Cycle 239's 268/268, 2257/2257 baseline). See roadmap row below.**

**Last updated: 2026-07-30 (Cycle 239 — extended Cycle 238's finding by re-auditing the *same* controller with a reproducible AST script (walking every `@Get`/`@Post`/`@Patch`/`@Delete` handler via the TypeScript compiler API, not grep) instead of assuming "8 routes" was the whole gap. It wasn't: **39 more undecorated routes** remained on `EmergencyOsController` alone, including the literal ED patient whiteboard (`getWhiteboard`), the patient journey timeline (`getJourney`), EMS inbound (`getEMS`), queues, reassessment, boarding, referrals, and 7 `upgrade-harness` clinical-intelligence routes — all confirmed by direct read of their backing services to call `EmergencyPatientService.listPatients()` and return real patient names/MRNs/timelines, not aggregate data (the whiteboard in particular is more severe than anything Cycle 238 found: it's the ED's primary patient-facing display). **Last updated: 2026-07-30 (Cycle 238 — a real, exploitable PHI-authorization gap on `EmergencyOsController`: 8 patient-tied routes (workflow logs, document artifacts, orchestration state, patient-flow-for-patient, clinical-calculator results ×2, copilot interactions ×2) carried no `@RequirePermission`/`@Permissions`/`@AnyPermission` decorator at all. `AuthorizationGuard` fails **open** whenever no permission metadata is present on a route (`if (!requiredPermissions && !anyPermissions) return true`) — confirmed by direct read of the guard, not assumed — so any authenticated user of any role, including STUDENT (the one backend role deliberately never granted `READ_PHI`/`WRITE_PHI`; `role-permissions.config.ts` scopes it to 5 educational-use clinical-tool permissions only, verified directly), could read or write another patient's real PHI and clinical-decision-support data through these 8 routes. Fixed by adding the decorator matching each route's actual data sensitivity: `READ_PHI` on the 4 patient-data-reading endpoints, `USE_CALCULATORS` on the 2 calculator-result endpoints, `USE_AI_CHAT` on the 2 copilot-interaction endpoints — both the permission enum values and the controller's existing `RequirePermission`/`Permission` imports verified real before use. New regression spec, `emergency-os-patient-endpoints-authorization.spec.ts`, inspects the actual `Reflect.getMetadata` the guard reads at request time (fails if a decorator is ever removed, or a future route joins this family without one) plus a static assertion that STUDENT's permission list can't satisfy the `READ_PHI` gate: **9/9 passing.** `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both 0 errors. Full backend suite: **268/268 suites, 2217/2217 tests, zero regressions** (+1 suite/+9 tests vs. Cycle 237's 267/267, 2208/2208 baseline). See roadmap item added this cycle (new "—" row, immediately below item #12).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-30 (Cycle 237 — re-verified roadmap item #2's "reentrancy/race issue" claim against the actual code before treating it as a real, live bug needing a design pass. `EmergencyPatientService` is in-memory-authoritative by design (its own code comment already says so — DB writes are fire-and-forget durability, reads never hit the DB); the question was whether concurrent requests could race on the in-memory array. Counted every `await` inside the class (grep across all ~694 lines): exactly 3, all confined to the one-time startup rehydration path (`onModuleInit`/`rehydrateBoardFromDatabase`) — every per-request read/write method (`createPatient`, `movePatientToState`, `patchPatient`, etc.) is fully synchronous, which Node's run-to-completion model makes atomic with respect to every other request. Confirmed via `docker-compose.app.yml` that this app's own deployment config runs a single backend instance (no `replicas`/cluster config), so the one theoretical multi-instance-divergence risk doesn't apply to how this repo actually deploys today. Conclusion: no live, exploitable race condition exists in the current single-instance deployment — the real reasons "Phase 2" (cutting reads to the DB) would matter are horizontal-scalability readiness and crash-durability, not an active bug. Rather than leave this safety implicit (and accidental — nothing stops a future edit from adding an `await` mid-method and silently reintroducing a real race), added a class-level doc comment stating the invariant and a new regression test (`emergency-patient-atomicity-invariant.spec.ts`) that mechanically enforces it — reads the real source, extracts the class body, asserts `await` never appears outside the allowed startup methods. Verified the guard actually catches a regression: temporarily made `movePatientToState` async with a real `await`, confirmed the test fails and names the offending method, reverted, confirmed green again. `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` both 0 errors. Full backend suite: 267/267 suites total, 1 failure on first run (`encryption.service.spec.ts`'s `expect(elapsed).toBeLessThan(100)` — a timing assertion under full-suite CPU contention) — isolated re-run came back clean, 1/1 suite, 37/37 tests, confirming the same pre-existing, environment-load-dependent flake this program has documented before (Cycle 222), unrelated to this cycle's change. Effective result: 267/267 suites, 2208/2208 tests, zero regressions attributable to this cycle (+1 suite/+1 test vs. Cycle 236's baseline). See roadmap item #2.**

**Last updated: 2026-07-29 (Cycle 227 — a real, HIPAA/GDPR-relevant compliance-reporting bug, found while re-checking whether Cycle 226's "org without packs is worse than no org" asymmetry pointed at a deeper entitlement problem (it didn't — subscription-tier gating already applies independently of organization status, a legitimate design, verified by direct code reading rather than assumed). While confirming the real onboarding flow correctly sets `profile.organizationId` for the completing user (it does, clean), found `ComplianceService.getConsentStatus()` — the real handler behind `GET /api/compliance/consent`, which `ConsentHistory.tsx`'s live `/legal/consent-history` page renders as the user's actual "Data processing (HIPAA / clinical use)" consent record — reports `dataProcessingConsent`, `termsAccepted`, and `privacyPolicyAccepted` all as `= user.emailVerified`, a boolean proxy, rather than reading `UserProfile.consentDataProcessing` — a real, dedicated column that the sibling `updateConsent()` method *does* correctly write to. A user who explicitly withdraws data-processing consent via `POST /api/compliance/consent` would see it reported as still granted on every subsequent read, as long as their email is verified (true for essentially every real user — OAuth and dev-session both auto-verify, and standard registration requires it). Also found `marketingConsent` hardcoded to `false` with a comment admitting it was a known placeholder ("would be stored in profile"), and `thirdPartySharingConsent` — a third real, actively-written consent column — omitted from the response entirely, invisible to the user on this compliance page. Fixed by fetching the user's profile relation and reading `consentDataProcessing`/`consentMarketingCommunications`/`consentThirdPartySharing` directly; left `termsAccepted`/`privacyPolicyAccepted` on the `emailVerified` proxy since no dedicated column exists for either (adding one is a schema change, out of scope for this fix) — documented why in a comment so the asymmetry reads as a deliberate limitation, not an oversight. Added the newly-available `thirdPartySharingConsent` field to `ConsentHistory.tsx`'s rendered list too, closing the loop on the frontend, not just the backend. The existing test for `getConsentStatus` had itself encoded the bug (asserting `dataProcessingConsent: true` derived from `emailVerified`, never mocking a profile at all) — rewrote it to mock a realistic profile with mixed true/false consent values and assert the real per-field behavior; confirmed the old code's return type didn't even have `thirdPartySharingConsent` as a compile-time proof of the gap. Full backend suite: **263/263 suites, 2201/2201 tests, zero regressions** (+1 net test). `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors, frontend and backend. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-29 (Cycle 226 — closed the anomaly Cycle 225 traced but didn't fix: `GET /api/platform/assets` returning 0 results for a real dev-session org, root-caused to `reason: 'pack-required'` on every asset. Chased *why* rather than accepting "expected dev-org gating" at face value — found `AuthService.ensureDevTenantForUser()` (the function `createDevSession` calls, described by Cycle 201 as "the real supported local/demo entry point") creates a real Organization + OrganizationMembership + Workspace, but never installs a single pack entitlement, unlike the actual customer-facing onboarding flow (`OrganizationOnboardingService.completeOnboarding()`), which always installs `core-platform` plus the org type's other defaults via `installPackForOrganization()`. Since `EntitlementService.resolveDecisionFromContext()`'s pack-required check (`missingPack`) applies whenever an organization exists *at all* — regardless of whether it owns any packs — a dev-session user ends up **more locked out than a plain org-less self-registered user**: nearly every asset, including FREE-tier core ones like `clinical-tools-core`, resolved to `isLaunchable:false`/`reason:'pack-required'`. This directly undermines dev-session's whole purpose as a frictionless demo/QA entry point, and is exactly the mechanism behind Cycle 225's "0 assets" finding. Fixed by adding `ensureDevOrganizationPacksInstalled()`, called unconditionally (idempotent, matching `installPackForOrganization`'s own idempotent create-or-reactivate semantics) inside `ensureDevTenantForUser` — installs `DEFAULT_PACKS_BY_ORGANIZATION_TYPE[HOSPITAL]` (`core-platform`, `reception-desk`, `emergency-medicine`, `laboratory-intelligence`, `hospital-operations`), the same defaults a real hospital signup gets. Wired `PlatformAssetsModule` into `AuthModule` and injected `PlatformAssetsService` as `@Optional()` (matching the existing `EmailService` pattern) to avoid a hard dependency; verified directly that no circular-module-dependency exists in the real Nest DI graph (Jest's `Test.createTestingModule` bypasses the real module graph entirely, so this needed an actual server boot, not just a passing test suite) by rebuilding and booting the compiled server for real — it started clean. Verified the fix live end-to-end against the *same pre-existing, already-broken* dev org from Cycle 225's session (packs are installed unconditionally, not gated behind "org just created," so the fix self-heals a pre-existing broken tenant too): `GET /api/platform/assets` went from 0 to 11 real launchable assets; `GET /api/platform/context` confirmed `entitledPackIds` now correctly lists all 5 default packs, `calculators` flipped from `reason:'pack-required'` to `isLaunchable:true`/`reason:'allowed'`, and — confirming no unintended side effect — `clinical-documentation-assistant` (Cycle 222's institutional-tier-gated fix) correctly remained `subscription-required`, since pack ownership and subscription tier are separate, both-still-enforced axes. Added 2 new regression tests (the happy-path pack-installation assertion, and an optional-dependency-absent safety check); verified red-before/green-after via `git stash`. Full backend suite: **263/263 suites, 2200/2200 tests, zero regressions** (+2 tests). `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 223 — a fresh full dependency/security re-audit (both trees, first since Cycle 190) found nothing newly actionable: frontend unchanged (2 moderate `react-router`, 24 high `brace-expansion`-chain — item #19/#21); backend's high count moved 34→38, but tracing each new name (`typeorm`, `@nestjs/cli`, `@typescript-eslint/*`, `create-jest`) found they're all the *same* already-documented `brace-expansion` DoS advisory, just newly reached through a 4th independent path (`typeorm`'s own bundled `glob`) — `npm audit fix --dry-run` confirms no version of `typeorm` resolves clean, same "no safe path" conclusion as the other 3 chains. Not the cycle's lever on its own, but folded into item #21's note. Picked up roadmap item #18 instead — re-verified Cycle 191's "7 fields have no home in the canonical interface" claim first rather than trusting a 32-cycle-old finding, and found it's now stale: all 7 (`phone`, `healthCardNumber`, `healthCard`, `source`, `lastAssessedTime`, `quickSafetyFlags`, `waitingRoomStatus`) are declared on `Patient` in `src/types/emergency.ts` today (added by unrelated work sometime after Cycle 191). But the type declaring a field turned out not to guarantee it's honored: `EmergencyPatientService.createPatient()` builds its return value from a hand-picked object literal covering only ~28 of the type's ~50+ fields — `ensurePatientArrivalBlock()`/`syncPatientFromArrival()` already preserve every field the caller sent via their own spread, but `createPatient`'s literal only copies a subset of that back out, silently dropping everything else. Confirmed real, live reachability, not just a type-level gap: `SmartIntake.tsx` sends `phone`/`healthCardNumber` from OCR-extracted document fields, `QuickIntake.tsx` sends `healthCard`/`waitingRoomStatus`/`source` directly in its create payload — both flow straight through `SmartIntakeCreateInput = Partial<EmergencyPatient>` to `createPatient()` with no DTO validation in between (item #18's other still-open half) to strip them first. `updatePatient()` was checked and confirmed to *not* share this bug — it already builds its result via `{...current, ...normalizedPatch}`, a spread-first pattern; only `createPatient`'s literal-construction was the outlier. Fixed by changing `createPatient` to spread `normalized` first, then override only the ~16 fields that genuinely need defaulting/normalization logic (id, mrn, name fields, dob, age, sex, timestamps, state, priority, vitals, flags, notes, timeline) — every other field, present or future, now survives creation the same way it already did through `updatePatient`. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 222 — followed up Cycle 221's entitlement/feature-flag audit with the one axis it hadn't checked: whether the `assetIds` referenced by `FEATURE_FLAG_REGISTRY`/`ENTITLEMENT_REGISTRY` actually match real, canonical asset ids anywhere else in the app, not just each other. The real effect: these 2 assets silently bypassed their intended `requiredPlan`/`requiredPackIds`/BETA-flag gating entirely (any user, any tier, unrestricted access) rather than being hidden — a fail-open entitlement-bypass bug, not a visibility bug. Fixed by correcting the stale ids to their real canonical ones in both `FEATURE_FLAG_REGISTRY` and `ENTITLEMENT_REGISTRY`, on both the frontend and backend copies (4 files, 6 string edits). See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 218 — followed up Cycle 217's `admin`/`it_admin` alias-shadowing fix with a systematic audit for the same bug *shape* elsewhere in `canonicalAccess.ts`'s alias table, using the standalone-real-source-execution technique unlocked last cycle. Wrote a script checking, for all 23 catalog roles, that every self-key and every explicitly-declared `aliases` entry actually resolves back to its own role — 70 checks total. Found exactly one more: `demo_observer` declares `'read-only-viewer'` as its own alias, but `lab_technician` (earlier in the catalog, sharing the identical `emergencyRoleId: 'read_only_viewer'`) auto-derives an alias that normalizes to the same string and silently won instead — same bug shape as Cycle 217's finding (an explicit declaration shadowed by an incidental one), different mechanism (catalog order, not a hardcoded seed this time). Confirmed the string is currently unreachable from any real caller in this codebase (only appears in this one declaration), unlike the `admin` case's live production reach — lower urgency, but still worth fixing rather than leaving a declared-but-broken alias as a landmine for whoever adds a UI surface for it later. Rather than patch this one string, fixed the actual mechanism: restructured the alias table's single-pass reduce (`if (!acc[key])`-once, so priority was really just "whichever claim ran first") into two explicit priority passes — every role's own self-key and declared `aliases` register first (deliberate, authored intent), then auto-derived `emergencyRoleId`/`saasRole` fallbacks only fill gaps left open. This fixes the `demo_observer` case, forecloses the entire bug class for any future catalog addition, and needed no per-string seed hack (the exact mechanism that caused the original `admin` bug). Verified rigorously before trusting the restructuring: captured a full snapshot of `resolveHospitalRole()` across all 80 meaningfully-distinct inputs (every self-key, declared alias, `emergencyRoleId`, and `saasRole` value across the whole catalog) both before and after, and diffed them — confirmed the *only* 2 lines that changed were the intended fix (`'read-only-viewer'`/`'read_only_viewer'` flipping from `lab_technician` to `demo_observer`), zero other resolutions affected. Also confirmed the flip is a net safety improvement, not just neutral: `demo_observer`'s `dataMinimizationLevel` is `metadata_only` (most restrictive), landing an inherently-ambiguous shared string there instead of `lab_technician`'s more permissive `clinical` level is the safer default. Added a new regression test capturing both the specific case and the general priority principle; re-verified all of `canonicalAccess.test.ts`'s pre-existing alias-resolution assertions (11 cases, including Cycle 217's `admin`/`platform-admin`/`it-admin` fixes) still pass unchanged. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors on the 2 touched files. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 217 — closed roadmap item #24 (opened last cycle): found, root-caused, and fixed the real `admin`-role command-palette bug, and confirmed it's a live production defect, not just a QA-fixture artifact. First unlocked a genuinely new verification technique this sandbox had never had before: Node 24's `--experimental-strip-types`/`--experimental-transform-types` flags plus a small custom module-resolution loader (registered via `--experimental-loader`, not `--import` — the latter silently doesn't register hook exports, which is why an earlier attempt last cycle failed) let real, unmodified `.ts` source execute standalone, resolving this codebase's extensionless imports and JSON imports along the way. Building the *actual* `compiledProfile` the app builds at runtime and inspecting it directly found the real bug one layer up: `getCanonicalRoleMapping('admin')` resolves to the **`it_admin`** catalog entry, not `super_admin` — traced to a single line in `canonicalAccess.ts`'s alias-seed table, `admin: 'it_admin'`, silently shadowing `super_admin`'s own explicit `aliases: ['admin', 'super-admin', 'platform-admin']` declaration (the seed's `if (!acc[key])` guard sets once and never yields, regardless of catalog order). `git blame` showed both lines were added in the *same* commit (`bb18f2b7`, 2026-06-28) — a genuine internal inconsistency, not an intentional restriction: `it_admin`'s own alias list is `['it-admin', 'technical-admin']`, never `'admin'`. Verified precisely: re-ran the same real-source trace and confirmed `compiledProfile.role.hospitalRole` for `'admin'` flips from `it_admin` to `super_admin`, the final resolved role flips from `it_admin` to `admin`, `presentEmergencyPermission` flips from `hidden` to `allowed`, and `routeAccess` grows from 45 to 48 routes — while re-running all 12 of `canonicalAccess.test.ts`'s own existing alias-resolution cases (nurse, rn, physician, triage_nurse, it-admin, it_admin, technical-admin, platform-admin, super-admin, ems-user, unknown-role-fallback) confirmed zero regressions. One existing test (`resolveHospitalRole('admin')).toBe('it_admin')`) had itself encoded the bug rather than the intent — inconsistent with its own sibling test's "first in catalog wins" comment — corrected alongside the fix. Confirmed this is a live production concern, not just a QA artifact: `backend/src/modules/emergency-os/emergency-os-onboarding.defaults.ts` maps real hospital-admin onboarding labels (`'clinic-administrator'`, `'hospital-administrator'`) directly to this same `'admin'` emergency role — meaning real onboarded hospital administrators, not just the QA fixture, have been silently restricted to IT-admin-only, metadata-only access (no AI copilot, no patient creation, no clinical actions) since 2026-06-28. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors on the 2 touched files. See Cycle log below).**

A full grep sweep (not an assumption) found 4 more independent copies of the identical bug (`perf-trace.mjs`, `perf-profile.mjs`, `perf-cls-sources.mjs`, `production-smoke.spec.mjs`) — fixed all 5, each verified red-before/green-after via a standalone script. With the suite genuinely executing, 12 of 15 pages came back clean and 3 real violations were found and fixed: a `role="list"` container around real `role="button"` toggle controls on the BMI calculator hub (`aria-required-children`, critical — fixed with `role="group"`), an unlabeled-in-the-accessibility-tree EMS unit-track graphic (`aria-prohibited-attr`, serious — fixed with `role="progressbar"` + real `aria-value*` attributes), and a 3.76:1 white-on-red "Escalate" button on the triage queue (`color-contrast`, serious — fixed with the same already-verified `--cdl-danger-600`/4.83:1 red Cycle 213 used). Verified all 3 fixes with real `@axe-core/playwright` scans against isolated before/after DOM fixtures (the stale `dist/` used to run the live suite predates all 3 fixes) — confirmed the exact violation fires before and is clean after, for each. Added 3 new source-text regression tests. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` (frontend + all 8 touched e2e/script files) all 0 errors. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 213 — closed out the 2 `__cta` candidates Cycles 207-208 left honestly unverified (`reception-job-profile-card__cta` on `/profile`, `reception-skill-strip__cta` on `/emergency/reception`), and found this was blocked by something more fundamental than the demo-auth issue Cycle 208 diagnosed. Root-caused the demo-auth failure precisely first: `scripts/capture-reception-screenshots.mjs`'s seeded `localStorage` payload had no `authMode`/`demoPersona` marker, so `demoPersonaModel.ts`'s `isDemoPersonaUser()` never recognized it as a demo session — `hydrateStoredDemoUser()` silently discarded the whole payload (including the `role` field the script was trying to set) and fell back to the app's default role every time, for every role the script ever requested. Proved this precisely with a standalone reconstruction of the real hydration logic (not guessed) and fixed the script by adding `authMode: 'open-access'` to its seeded payload. But then found the fix alone still couldn't render either CTA: `ReceptionJobProfileCard.tsx`/`ReceptionSkillStrip.tsx` were both added 2026-07-24 — 5 days after `dist/`'s last build (2026-07-19) — so the served static bundle doesn't contain this code *at all* (confirmed: their class names appear nowhere in `dist/assets/*.js`). This is a harder blocker than dist staleness elsewhere in this program (which affects only whether a *fix* is reflected, not whether a *feature* exists at all), so verification switched to a new technique: a hand-built HTML harness loading the real, current source CSS files directly via native browser `@import`/`<link>` (no bundler needed — sidesteps the blocked Vite/esbuild path entirely) against markup matching each component's real JSX, run through the same Playwright+system-Edge pipeline used throughout this program. Measured a real, severe WCAG failure — **~1.02:1** (worse than every prior instance of this bug) — confirming the identical sweep-rule footgun a third time: `color-normalization.css`'s `[class*='cta']` sweep and `text-normalization.css`'s `__cta`/plain-anchor rules both don't recognize either class as an already-solid, already-white-text button. Fixed with the same targeted `:not()` exclusions as Cycles 207-208 (re-measured live: 4.10:1, still short of AA). That surfaced a second, genuinely separate, pre-existing defect once the sweep-rule masking was removed: both components' own intended background token, `--cdl-brand-600`, resolves to `#0284c7` in light mode (4.10:1 with white text) and `#0ea5e9` in dark mode (2.77:1) — neither passes WCAG AA's 4.5:1, and dark mode is worse than light. Fixed by switching both components' CTA background to a fixed, theme-constant `#075985` (this codebase's existing `--cdl-info-text`/`--medical-sky-600` value) rather than a theme-swapping token — verified live in both themes: **7.56:1** for both, unaffected by the reception-skill-strip's existing `--critical` red variant (already-passing 4.83:1, untouched). Added a new regression suite section (`fjCriticalBannerCta.contrast.test.ts`, 5 more assertions) proven red-without/green-with via a standalone script. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` (frontend + the fixed script) all 0 errors. New verification technique added to the toolkit: a raw-HTML-plus-real-source-CSS harness, for exactly the case where source has outpaced the only available compiled artifact enough that no amount of dist-patching can substitute. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 211 — continued roadmap item #22's route-audit batch (running total 141 of 179 going in), screenshotting a fresh batch of 14 previously-unaudited routes (`/auth/forgot-password`, `/auth/magic-link`, `/auth/invite`, `/surveillance/nexus`, `/digital-twin-intelligence`, `/customer-portal`, `/knowledge-base`, `/enterprise-readiness`, `/trackmind`, `/trackmind-maturity`, `/enterprise-platform`, `/platform-intelligence`, `/platform-admin`, `/workflow-mining` — running total now **155 of 179**). Most fell back cleanly to the shared reception/settings/tools pages (expected, consistent with every prior cycle's findings on not-yet-role-wired routes); `/workflow-mining` is a real, distinct page.

Found a real, clearly-reproducible bug on `/workflow-mining`'s "Journey frequency" chart: its diagonal (rotated -20°) x-axis category labels were visibly clipped, e.g. "ED triage calculator to dashboard" rendered as "...ulator to dashboard." Investigated this thoroughly since the shared `CategoryBarChart` component (`src/components/dashboard/DashboardCharts.tsx`) it comes from is used by **42 real pages** across the app — worth understanding precisely before touching something this widely shared. Root-caused to a genuinely multi-layered interaction, not a single simple bug: **(1)** the chart's `margin={{..., left: 0}}` gives rotated, `textAnchor="end"` labels no reserved space to extend into as they swing left; **(2)** independently, the shared `.dashboard-visual-panel` container (already touched once before, Cycle 205) also clips via `overflow: hidden`; **(3)** the app shell's own top-level scroll containers (`app-shell-main-content`, `.emergency-app-shell`) contribute a further, non-negotiable clip boundary that cannot safely be touched at all. Attempted two fix directions and verified each empirically rather than assuming success: increasing the chart's internal `margin.left` (tested via the compiled-JS-literal-patch-and-restore technique, mirroring the established CSS dist-patch pipeline) shifted the plot's internal layout as expected but had non-linear, hard-to-predict side effects — at a large enough value to meaningfully help, category bands compressed enough that adjacent rotated labels started visually overlapping each other, a new problem trading one clipping bug for another. Separately, overriding the shared panel/chart container's `overflow: hidden` to `visible` (tested via an injected style, not the compiled bundle) fixed 2 of the 3 labels completely with no visible side effects, but the 3rd (leftmost, longest-reaching) label still clipped against the untouchable app-shell boundary. Concluded a *safe*, fully-verified fix isn't achievable this cycle: this component's 42-consumer blast radius means a fix needs verification across a real, representative sample of very differently-shaped label data — something this sandbox's blocked JS rebuild path can't support with confidence beyond literal-value bundle patching, and the interacting-but-partial nature of both attempted fixes (each solving part of the problem while risking a different regression) is exactly the kind of finding that shouldn't be shipped half-understood. Documented the full root-cause chain and both partial fix attempts for a future, dedicated cycle rather than force a partial or risky change — the same discipline as Cycle 208's 2 honestly-left-unverified `__cta` candidates. No source files changed this cycle; `git status` confirmed clean before and after besides the two pre-existing untracked concurrent-session directories. See Cycle log below).**

Found and fixed a genuine, previously-uncaught crash on `/legal/consent-history`: the page threw to the app's ErrorBoundary whenever consent status couldn't be loaded (which is unconditional in this static-serve environment, and would also be real whenever a live backend's `/api/compliance/consent` endpoint is unreachable or the `complianceConsent` capability is disabled). Root-caused precisely with a real React server-render reproduction, not guessed: `ConsentHistory.tsx`'s error-state and empty-state branches both called the shared `EmptyState` component with `action={{ label: 'Try Again', onClick: fetchConsentHistory }}` — a plain JavaScript object — but `EmptyState` (confirmed both real implementations in this codebase, `components/ui/EmptyState.tsx` and `components/data-display/EmptyState.tsx`, share the identical contract) renders `action` directly as a child (`{action}`), and React throws `"Objects are not valid as a React child"` for a plain object rather than a `ReactNode`. Also found a second, quieter bug in the same two call sites: `message={error}` was passed instead of `EmptyState`'s real `description` prop, meaning even once the crash is fixed, the actual error text would have silently never appeared (spread onto the DOM as an unrecognized attribute instead). Fixed both call sites by passing a real `<button>` element for `action` and renaming `message` → `description`. Verified with a genuine `react-dom/server` reproduction (not a description of the bug, an actual render): the exact pre-fix JSX shape throws the exact real error message seen live; the exact post-fix shape renders clean HTML with the error text now genuinely visible. Found this component had zero prior test coverage at all — added `ConsentHistory.test.tsx` (3 tests: the disabled-capability error path, the fetch-failure error path, and a real successful render with 4 consent records) via `@testing-library/react`, mocking only the 2 real service/config dependencies. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors, full frontend scope — the new test file typechecking cleanly is itself a partial confirmation the fix's prop shapes are correct, on top of the SSR proof. See Cycle log below).**

Found and fixed 2 real, substantial bugs on `/live-map` (Live Tracking Map): **(1)** every card in the "Tracked assets" list ran its title, meta line, and subtitle together with zero space (`.live-map__marker-item`'s children — `<strong>`/`<span>`/`<small>`, all inline by default — had no stacking layout at all), reading as "North routeFLEET · occupiedoccupied · CareDroid North Clinic · ETA 18m"; fixed with `display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 2px` on the item, confirmed live via dist-patch that the 3 lines now render as intended, separate rows. **(2)** the map canvas's SVG marker labels ("F"/"I") rendered as enormous letters roughly 7x the diameter of their own marker dot — traced to `<text x="2.8" y="1">{...}</text>` in `LiveTrackingMap.tsx` never having had a `font-size` set anywhere, so it fell back to the browser's ~16px default text size interpreted directly as SVG user units inside a 0-100 `viewBox`, next to a radius-2.2 circle; fixed with an explicit `font-size: 2.4px` (SVG user units) on `.live-map-canvas__marker text`, confirmed live via the same dist-patch pipeline that markers now render as small, legible glyphs beside their dots. Separately found the *word*-level half of the concatenation bug — "occupiedoccupied" isn't just missing a space, `occupied` is genuinely repeated — traced to `liveTrackingMapModel.ts`'s `buildFleetTrackingMarkers`: `subtitle: [FLEET_STATUS_TONE[vehicle.status] ? vehicle.status : vehicle.status, ...]` is a tautological ternary (both branches identical), so it unconditionally puts `vehicle.status` first in the subtitle — redundant with the marker-list UI's own `<span>{marker.layer.toUpperCase()} · {marker.status}</span>`, rendered immediately before it. Fixed by deleting the dead ternary and the redundant status entirely from the subtitle (`FLEET_STATUS_TONE` remains meaningfully used elsewhere, for marker-tone coloring, so nothing orphaned); since this is a JS/TS logic change (blocked JS rebuild path), verified via a standalone Node script reconstructing both the exact old and new logic and running all 4 real vehicle statuses from the actual screenshot through both — old always duplicated the status at the seam, new never does. Added 2 new tests to the existing `liveTrackingMapModel.test.ts`. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors, full frontend scope. See Cycle log below).**

Found and fixed a genuine WCAG contrast failure on a safety-critical control: the "Acknowledge" button for unacknowledged critical alerts (`.fj-critical-banner__cta` in `FullJourneyOperatingPage.tsx`, live on 4 real routes — `ed-readiness`, `diagnostics`, `handoffs`, `reports`) rendered at **~1.41:1 contrast** (dark teal text on dark navy background) instead of its own deliberately-designed white-on-red styling — measured precisely via `getComputedStyle` and the real WCAG relative-luminance formula, not eyeballed. Root-caused to *three* independent app-wide CSS sweep rules all matching this one element because its class contains the literal substring "cta": `color-normalization.css`'s `[class*='cta']` sweep (repaints background+color for anything "CTA-like"), plus *two* separate rules in `text-normalization.css` (a plain `a:not([class*='btn'])...` anchor-color rule, and a `[class*='__cta']:not(--solid):not(--primary))` rule assuming solid buttons are always BEM-flagged accordingly) — all three fired because this component doesn't follow the assumed `--solid`/`--primary` naming convention despite genuinely being a solid-filled button. This is the identical substring-match sweep-rule footgun class Cycle 199 first diagnosed (`[class*='--primary']` catching `.cdl-zone--primary-actions`), now confirmed recurring across a second, independent selector family in the same file. Fixed by adding `:not(.fj-critical-banner__cta)` to all three rules, restoring the component's own intended styling — verified live via the dist-patch-and-restore pipeline (contrast measured 1.41:1 before, **6.47:1 after**, comfortably clearing WCAG AA's 4.5:1 threshold), with a fresh zoomed screenshot confirming the button now reads clearly. Added a 4-assertion regression test (`fjCriticalBannerCta.contrast.test.ts`) covering all 3 exclusions plus the component's own unchanged styling, proven red-without/green-with the fix via a standalone script since Vitest remains blocked. While investigating, also found `.profile-copilot-card__cta` on `/profile` live-measured with the identical broken color pair — but tracing its root cause showed its background half is entangled with a different, deliberate light-muted-surface design intent (not a critical-red case like this fix), needing its own visual judgment call rather than a mechanical copy of this fix — flagged, not fixed, this cycle. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors, full frontend scope. See Cycle log below).**

Correctly took no action on this finding — recorded it as a confirmed non-issue rather than either a silent skip or a redundant fix.

Two real, verified bugs found and fixed in the same batch. **First**: `/emergency/settings`'s "3-minute target" risk indicator rendered as a 711px-tall red box holding just 3 short lines of text, spread apart with huge gaps instead of grouped compactly — root-caused via live `getComputedStyle` measurement to a genuine CSS Grid default-value interaction: `.bottleneck-risk` (`src/components/bottlenecks/BottleneckPanels.tsx`'s `ThreeMinuteRiskIndicator`) sits in a 3-column grid (`.emergency-settings__bottleneck-admin`) alongside two content-heavy sibling columns; the grid's default `align-items: stretch` forces this narrow card to match its tallest sibling's height, and since `.bottleneck-risk` is itself `display: grid` with no `align-content` set, CSS Grid's *own* default value (`align-content: stretch`) then spread its 3 short `auto`-sized rows (label/status/summary) to fill that entire forced height — measured each row inflating to ~224px for text that needs ~25px. Fixed with one line, `align-content: start`, verified live via the dist-CSS-patch-and-restore pipeline: measured each row collapse from ~224px back to its natural 23-35px, then confirmed visually via a fresh screenshot that all 3 lines now sit compactly at the top of the card exactly like its sibling columns, with the remaining height simply unused background (a normal, common "some cards are taller than their content" pattern, not a broken one). **Second**: `/emergency/referrals` showed a visible double-period typo — "...authentication redirect.. Showing the last local referral queue..." — from `src/components/ReferralPanel.tsx` literally concatenating `{referralsModule.error}` (an `apiClient.ts`-sourced message that already ends in its own period) with a literal `. Showing the last local referral queue...` suffix. Fixed by stripping any existing trailing period(s) before appending (`.replace(/\.+\s*$/, '')`), verified via a standalone script proving correct single-period punctuation across 4 real message shapes this codebase actually produces (some ending in a period, some not) — a low-risk, purely additive fix; confirmed no test anywhere asserts the old text. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors, full frontend scope. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 205 — continued roadmap item #22, screenshotting a fresh batch of 14 previously-unaudited routes (`workflows`, `search`, `feature-flags`, `system-health`, `governance-registry`, `human-review`, `training`, `costs`, `brain`, `organization-intelligence`, `competencies`, `credentials`, `clinical-decision-support`, `predictive-analytics` — running total now **100 of 182** real routes). Found a real, reproducible chart-rendering bug affecting at least 2 pages: `/competencies` and `/credentials` both render a "status mix" bar chart whose entire body was invisible — the panel showed only its title/description then cut straight to the next section, with just 1-2 bare Y-axis gridline labels visible. Root-caused via direct DOM/computed-style inspection rather than guessing: the chart's real SVG (Recharts, `width=100% height={240}` explicitly set) genuinely renders in both cases, but its ancestor `.dashboard-visual-panel` — a CSS Grid item with `overflow: hidden` — was computing an actual height of just **42px** on `/competencies` (and 158px, still clipped, on `/credentials`), because per the CSS Grid/Flexbox spec, a grid item with `overflow: hidden` gets an *implied automatic minimum size of 0* rather than sizing to its content's natural height — confirmed by comparing against a working chart (`/costs`'s "Route mix", correctly 316px) whose panel happens to sit inside an intermediate chart-specific grid wrapper (`.cost-page__charts`) that the 2 broken pages don't have; both broken panels are instead *direct* items of their page's single top-level grid, where the collapse manifests. Fixed at the shared component level — `src/components/dashboard/DashboardVisualizations.css`'s `.dashboard-visual-panel` rule — by adding `.dashboard-visual-panel:has(.dashboard-chart) { min-height: 300px; }`, scoped specifically to chart-bearing panels (verified `:has()` is already an established pattern in this codebase, used in 5+ other files) so panels without a chart are completely unaffected; the 300px floor was derived from the chart wrapper's own fixed 210px height plus its header/description/padding, matching the real working panel's ~316px closely. This is a CSS-only fix, so — unlike the last 2 fixes in this thread — it could go through the full, established dist-CSS-patch-and-restore verification technique: patched the exact compiled selector into the built `dist/assets/DashboardVisualizations-*.css`, reloaded both pages, confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that the panel height jumped from 42px/158px to a full 300px and the parent grid row resized to match, then took fresh screenshots confirming both charts now render with real, visible, correctly-colored bars and x-axis category labels exactly like every other working chart on the platform — restored the build artifact byte-for-byte afterward (diffed to confirm). This fix applies at the shared component CSS level, so it protects all 36 real pages using `VisualizationPanel` in this codebase, not just the 2 confirmed-broken ones — the other ~34 were not individually re-audited this cycle to count how many were coincidentally already safe by virtue of their specific grid nesting. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors, full frontend scope (a CSS-only change, so this confirms nothing else broke rather than validating the fix itself — the live dist-patch screenshot is what validates the fix). See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 204 — continued roadmap item #22, screenshotting a fresh batch of 14 previously-unaudited routes (`dashboard`, `intake`, `queue`, `triage`, `alerts`, `staff`, `analytics`, `reports`, `protocols`, `research`, `laboratory`, `simulation`, `devices`, `timeline` — running total now **86 of 182** real routes). Then found a genuinely new, higher-severity bug via a different investigation thread: `/alerts` showed real situation-brief numbers ("13 active alerts," "13 need acknowledgement") but rendered *no alert list at all* below them — reading `ClinicalAlertsPage.tsx`'s source confirmed it builds a real, rich `activeWork` block (summary chips, search, controls, the actual alert cards) and passes it to the shared `EmergencyRoutePage` wrapper via an `activeWork={...}` prop. Root-caused precisely: `EmergencyRoutePage` (`src/pages/emergency/emergencyRouteShared.tsx`) never declared an `activeWork` prop at all — only `children` — and since the whole props object is typed `any`, nothing caught the mismatch; the content was silently dropped every time.
