Fixed at the root rather than patching either call site: added `activeWork` as a real, recognized prop on `EmergencyRoutePage` that falls back to `children` (`activeWork ?? children`) — confirmed via a direct check that neither broken call site also passes `children` (both are self-closing JSX tags), so this is a strictly additive fix with zero risk of duplicate/conflicting content. This one-line, two-file-blast-radius fix makes two previously-half-broken pages whole again with no changes needed to either page's own source. Added a new regression suite, `emergencyRouteShared.activeWork.test.tsx` (3 tests, `@testing-library/react`, mocking the component's real dependency hooks by their verified real export shapes — named vs. default — checked against source before writing the mocks, not assumed): confirms content passed via `activeWork` now renders (the fix), confirms content passed via `children` still renders identically (zero regression to the 4 other, already-correct call sites), and confirms neither-prop-given still correctly renders nothing (no false positive). Since frontend Vitest is still blocked by the same `esbuild` sandbox issue, supplemented with a standalone Node script proving the exact `activeWork ?? children` resolution across all 3 real-world call patterns byte-for-byte. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors, full frontend scope. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 203 — resumed roadmap item #22 (visual/UI polish) after 2 cycles on item #23, screenshotting a fresh batch of 14 previously-unaudited routes via the established static-`dist/`-serve + real-Edge pipeline (`billing`, `usage`, `notifications`, `security`, `audit`, `assets`, `ai-models`, `platform-analytics`, `department-intelligence`, `service-lines`, `product-intelligence`, `success-center`, `profile`, `help-center` — bringing the running total to **72 of the app's 182 real routes** (`qa/full-app-crawl/report.json`'s Cycle 154 crawl gives an exact figure now, tightening the prior "~190+" estimate). Found a real bug via a same-root-cause side-by-side comparison rather than a single page in isolation: `/notifications` rendered a raw, unstyled browser exception directly in the page — `Unexpected token '<', "<!DOCTYPE "... is not valid JSON` — while `/security` and `/audit`, hitting the exact identical underlying condition (this static-serve environment has no live backend, so every API call resolves to the SPA's own `index.html` fallback instead of JSON), showed a clean, designed message: "The API returned an HTML page instead of JSON. Check the API URL, proxy, backend availability, or authentication redirect." Root-caused, not just patched at the display layer: that friendly message comes from `src/services/apiClient.ts`'s shared `parseApiResponse` helper, which detects an HTML body/content-type *before* attempting `JSON.parse` and throws a designed `ApiResponseError` instead of raw `SyntaxError` — `/security`/`/audit`'s governance-dashboard component already uses it correctly, but `src/services/NotificationService.ts` was inconsistent: `getUnreadCount`, `markAllAsRead`, `fetchDevices`, and `toggleAllNotifications` already call `parseApiResponse` correctly, while 4 sibling methods on the exact same object — `fetchNotificationHistory`, `markAsRead`, `getPreferences`, `updatePreferences` — called raw `response.json()` directly, bypassing the HTML guard entirely and letting the browser's own `SyntaxError` propagate straight into `NotificationPreferences.tsx`'s `Promise.allSettled` error-surfacing path unguarded. Fixed all 4 by applying the file's own already-correct pattern (`const data = await parseApiResponse(response, {fallback:{}}); if (!response.ok) throw new Error(data?.message || getApiErrorMessage(null, response)); return data;`), zero invention — confirmed via a repo-wide grep that no test anywhere asserts the 4 hardcoded strings being replaced. While tracing test coverage for this fix, found the *same class of blind spot* Cycle 201 found in the AI-chat DTO: `src/test/NotificationService.test.ts` (the only file with a matching name) actually imports and tests an unrelated `./fixtures/legacyNotificationService` fixture, not this real service at all, and `NotificationPreferences.test.tsx` mocks `NotificationService` wholesale at the module boundary — meaning these 4 REST methods' real HTTP-parsing behavior had **zero real test coverage**, exactly why this bug was invisible. Wrote a new dedicated suite, `NotificationService.rest.test.ts` (6 tests), mocking only the actual network boundary (`apiFetch`) while keeping the real `parseApiResponse`/`getApiErrorMessage` logic live — the correct mock boundary for catching this exact bug class, unlike the existing component test's boundary. Since frontend Vitest is still blocked by the same `esbuild` sandbox issue (re-confirmed fresh) and even a plain `tsx` import of `apiClient.ts` hangs on its own dev-session/backend-reachability side effects outside a real browser, verified with a standalone Node script reconstructing `isProbablyHtml`/`parseApiResponse`/`ApiResponseError` verbatim from the real source: proved byte-for-byte that the OLD method logic reproduces the exact observed bug string, the NEW logic produces the exact friendly message already shown on `/security`/`/audit`, a genuine success response still parses and returns correctly (no regression), and a real backend-supplied error message on an actual 404 still surfaces correctly rather than being swallowed. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors, full frontend scope. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 202 — investigation-only follow-up to Cycle 201's finding, closing its natural next question: is the `stream`-field DTO-mismatch bug class isolated to one endpoint, or does it recur elsewhere in real AI traffic? Restarted the real compiled backend (same `NODE_ENV=production` + dev-auth-bypass configuration as Cycle 201) and checked the app's *other* "canonical" AI entry point, `POST /api/ai/node` (structured intent-based requests — `src/services/careDroidAiApi.ts`'s `transportCareDroidAINode`). Statically compared the frontend's `CareDroidAIRequest` TypeScript interface (`lib/ai/careDroidAITypes.ts`: exactly `intent`/`input`/`context`, nothing else) against the backend's `CareDroidAINodeDto` (`backend/src/modules/ai/dto/ai.dto.ts`: the identical 3 fields) — a clean 1:1 match, unlike the conversational endpoint's DTO, which was a hand-duplicated shape that had silently drifted from its own client's object-literal construction. Didn't stop at the static read: fired a real request through the real backend with a real `dev-session` JWT and the exact real payload shape — cleared validation with no 400, and reached the same real, legitimate `subscription-required (professional)` entitlement gate Cycle 201 already found and correctly left alone, confirming the request shape itself is genuinely fine. **Conclusion: the bug class doesn't recur here.** Root cause is now precise, not just "a DTO was wrong": it's specifically hand-duplicated request shapes with no compile-time link between a frontend object-literal builder and a backend DTO class — `/api/ai/node/conversational`'s DTO had that shape (independently hand-authored, drifted), `/api/ai/node`'s DTO doesn't (both sides reference/mirror the same 3-field shape). Also noticed, while transport-layer reading `careDroidAiApi.ts`, that `transportCareDroidAINode` has a try/catch that falls back to a local, frontend-only AI implementation (`runCareDroidAI`) on *any* backend failure — including a validation 400 like Cycle 201's — meaning a bug at this specific endpoint would have been additionally masked by looking like it "still works," just via a silent local fallback, rather than an obvious visible failure. Worth being aware of for any future investigation of this transport path, though not itself a bug (a deliberate, reasonable resilience pattern). Checking every one of the ~146 real POST/PATCH/PUT endpoints catalogued in `frontendApiCallsInventory.ts` for the same hand-duplication risk is out of scope for a single cycle — flagged as a pattern to stay alert to (added as a note on roadmap item #23) rather than claimed as exhaustively audited. No code changes this cycle; `git status` confirmed clean before and after besides the two pre-existing untracked concurrent-session directories. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 201 — per a direct request to "make sure the AI is interactive within the platform," did something no prior cycle in this 200-cycle program had ever done: drove a genuinely real, fully unmocked round trip — real compiled backend + real compiled frontend, real registration/dev-session bootstrap, real Playwright browser (system Edge) — through the Copilot chat, instead of relying on unit tests (which mock the HTTP layer), hermetic e2e specs (which stub the network at `page.route()` before any request reaches a real server), or static analysis. First confirmed something genuinely positive: the pre-existing `e2e/interactive-ai.spec.mjs` suite (IX12-14 — named streaming phases, keyboard-operable workflow-card Acknowledge, EMS inbox assign/comment collaboration, typed AI command-palette dispatch, offline/online resilience, 0 serious/critical a11y violations) had not been runnable since before Cycle 160's `esbuild` sandbox block appeared (last real run recorded 2026-07-18) — re-ran it fresh via the static-dist + real-Edge pipeline with `QA_REUSE_SERVER=true`, and it passed clean, 8/8, for the first time in ~10 days and dozens of cycles. Then went further: built and ran the actual backend (`nest build` + `node dist/backend/src/main.js`, unrelated to the blocked Vite/esbuild path — Nest's own build system was never affected), discovered it also serves the frontend's static `dist/` from the same origin when `NODE_ENV=production` (`registerProductionFrontendAssets` in `backend/src/main.ts`), and drove the two together for a real integration test never attempted before. Found and fixed a genuine, total, unconditional break in the core AI-chat feature: the browser client's real request body (`src/lib/ai/client.ts`'s `bodyForBackendRequest`) always includes a `stream` key — even `stream: false` — but the backend's `UnifiedNodeConversationalDto` (`backend/src/modules/chat/emergency-ai.controller.ts`) never declared it, and the app's global `ValidationPipe` (`whitelist:true, forbidNonWhitelisted:true`) rejects requests by property *presence*, not value — so **every single real call to `POST /api/ai/node/conversational` failed with 400 "property stream should not exist," unconditionally, streaming or not**, for every requestType this endpoint serves (Copilot chat, Smart Intake AI, referral summaries, analytics). Traced via `git log -S` to commit `9434922c` (2026-06-13) — this has been broken for 6+ weeks and ~120+ cycles, invisible to every prior test because the only existing spec touching this DTO (`emergency-ai.controller.spec.ts`) asserts against the *source text* (`readFileSync` + `toContain`), never against a real `class-validator` pass. Fixed by adding `stream?: boolean` to the DTO (`@IsOptional() @IsBoolean()`, matching the client's real intent — the field isn't wired to an actual SSE response at this endpoint yet, but declaring it unblocks real traffic without a larger streaming-implementation change). Wrote a genuine regression test, `emergency-ai-unified-node.dto.spec.ts`, that validates the *exact* real client payload shape through `class-validator` directly (not a source-text check) — confirmed it fails red without the fix (reverted it live, re-ran, watched it fail with the exact "stream should not exist" error, then restored) before trusting it. Continued past this fix to fully close the verification loop: with the DTO fixed, the same real request advanced from 400 → 403, surfacing a second, *legitimate, intentional* gate — `agent-clinical` requires `SubscriptionTier.PROFESSIONAL` per `backend/src/config/entitlements.config.ts`'s explicit, documented business-tier config (`featureFlagId: 'ai-clinical-copilot'`) — confirmed via source, not assumed, this is deliberate SaaS monetization, not a bug. Temporarily bumped one disposable local dev-DB test row to `professional` tier purely to prove the endpoint now resolves past validation and tier-gating into a third, also-legitimate `pack-required` entitlement check, then reverted the row byte-for-byte. Also separately confirmed a raw self-registration path (`/api/auth/register` + `/api/auth/login`) hits a *different*, expected dead end (`Organization context is required` — no org gets auto-provisioned for a bare self-registered account) — not a bug, since this app has no exposed login/registration UI at all (`deriveAuthMode`/`isRealAuthToken`/`isOpenAccessUser` in `src/auth/authSession.ts` are deliberately hardcoded post-"auth UI removal," per existing git history) — the real, intended local/demo entry point is `POST /api/auth/dev-session`, which *does* auto-provision a consistent real org+workspace+user and was what surfaced the actual, fixable bug. Verified: new DTO spec 1/1 (3/3, incl. the red/green proof), pre-existing `emergency-ai.controller.spec.ts` still 1/1, backend `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors, backend `eslint` 0 errors, full backend suite re-run clean (see below for count). Opened new roadmap item #23 documenting both the fix and the new full-stack-integration verification technique this cycle established. See Cycle log below).**

Since this is a small, fixed, fully-enumerable set of 6 hardcoded dimensions (not dynamic/user-generated data), fixed by adding an explicit `midSentenceLabel` field to each dimension with the already-correct casing, rather than reaching for a smarter-but-still-fragile string-transform heuristic (e.g. "leave the first word alone if it's 2+ consecutive capitals") — data that's simply correct by construction beats detection logic for a fixed, small set. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 199 — continued roadmap item #22, screenshotted 14 more real pages (44 of ~190+ routes now looked at across 4 cycles), and found the highest-blast-radius bug of the thread so far: a large, solid dark-navy rectangle with no content, consistently occupying the right portion of the "Critical actions" panel on every command-center-style page (Whiteboard, command-center, and the executive/admin fallback pages, which all share this panel). Diagnosed via DOM inspection, not assumed to be a missing-data artifact of the static-serve environment: the dark background belonged to `.cdl-zone--primary-actions` itself (`rgb(55,65,81)`/`#374151`), a layout-grid *section* wrapping the actual white "Critical actions" cards — meaning the section was painted dark everywhere the (few, demo-limited) cards didn't happen to cover it. Root-caused to a real, if not obviously visible, cross-cutting CSS bug rather than a per-page issue: `src/styles/color-normalization.css` has a deliberate, documented sweep rule (`role-accent-theme.css`'s own comment explains it) styling every BEM `--primary` button modifier dark navy via `[class*='--primary']` — a plain substring match with no word-boundary check — and `cdl-zone--primary-actions` (a layout *zone* name, not a button) happens to contain that exact substring, so the sweep silently painted the whole zone's background instead of just its intended button children. Confirmed via grep that this was the only accidental victim among 15 real classes containing `--primary` in the codebase (14 are genuine button/CTA modifiers the rule is correctly meant to catch) — and separately checked the rule's sibling `[class*='cta']` selector for the same collision risk, finding none (every `*cta*` class in the codebase is a genuine call-to-action, no accidental substring hits). Fixed with a targeted `:not(.cdl-zone--primary-actions)` exclusion, following the exact pattern the rule already uses for its one previously-known false positive (`:not(.reception-desk-toolbar__action)`) — a one-line, minimal, precisely-scoped fix that doesn't touch the deliberate invariant itself. Verified with the same dist-patch-and-screenshot technique as Cycles 196-198: patched the exact compiled selector in the built CSS, reloaded the Whiteboard page, and confirmed via both `getComputedStyle` (background now `rgba(0,0,0,0)`, fully transparent) and a live screenshot that the dark rectangle is completely gone, replaced by normal page background around the "Critical actions" card. Restored the build artifact byte-for-byte afterward. `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 198 — continued roadmap item #22, screenshotted a further 12 real pages (30 of ~190+ routes now looked at across 3 cycles) and found a third real visual bug, this time a mid-word text-wrap break rather than a hard clip: on the Clinical Calculator Hub (`/tools/calculators`), category badges like "GENERAL" rendered as "GENERA" on one line and a lone "L" on the next. Root-caused via computed styles, not guessed: `.clinical-calculator-card__category` (`practitioner-compact.css`) is a sibling of the card's `<strong>` title inside a `display: flex` header row (`.clinical-calculator-card__header`), and — unlike the title, which has natural word-break points and already wraps gracefully — the badge had no `flex-shrink: 0`, so on cards with a long title (e.g. "ACS Workflow Assistant") it got squeezed by the flex algorithm below its own natural content width (measured: 44px, well under what "GENERAL" needs at 11px/700-weight/uppercase), and with `overflow-wrap: break-word` already active and no space to break at, it split mid-letter instead. Fixed with `flex-shrink: 0; white-space: nowrap` on the badge, so it always renders at its natural width and the already-flexible title absorbs any space pressure instead (the correct direction, since word-wrapping a multi-word title looks intentional while breaking a single-word badge mid-letter never does). Verified with the same dist-patch-and-screenshot technique as Cycles 196-197: confirmed all 4 visible category badges on the page — including "RESPIRATORY", 11 characters, longer than "GENERAL" — rendered correctly on one line after the patch, restored the build artifact byte-for-byte afterward, and confirmed via grep this is the class's only definition anywhere in the codebase (no competing rule to reconcile). `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-28 (Cycle 196 — first cycle explicitly aimed at visual/UI polish rather than backend correctness, per a user request to hold the app to a genuinely high, "top-notch" design bar and keep scanning for gaps against it. This requires actually looking at the app, not just static analysis — so first re-confirmed, fresh, that `npm run dev` still hits the exact same `esbuild`/Application Control `spawn UNKNOWN` documented since Cycle 160 (real error captured this cycle: `vite.config.ts` fails to load, `ensureServiceIsRunning` → `esbuild/lib/main.js` → `ChildProcess.spawn` → `Error: spawn UNKNOWN`) — still fully blocked, not stale information. Found a real workaround that doesn't need Vite at all: served the existing (8-day-stale but structurally current) production build in `dist/` with a plain static file server, then drove the real system Edge browser via Playwright (`playwright-core` + `channel: msedge`, the same technique `scripts/audit-full-app-crawl.mjs` established) to actually screenshot 6 real pages. Found a genuine visual defect on the Whiteboard: the header's operational status-chip rail (`ChromeStatusChips.tsx` → `.alarm-kpi-rail` in `alarm-system.css`) is `overflow-x: auto` with no visual cue that it scrolls, so when its chips don't all fit (measured: 346px of content in a 288px-wide rail), the last chip gets hard-clipped by the container edge mid-word — confirmed via DOM measurement, not guesswork, that the label `"Breached"` (the correct string, per `resolveCompactHeaderMetricLabel`'s own test) renders visually as `"Breache"` with no ellipsis, no fade, nothing — reads exactly like a rendering bug, not "scroll for more." Fixed with a `mask-image` edge fade (fades alpha, not a background color, so it's correct in both themes and behind any content) — purely additive CSS, touches nothing else, can't regress layout or behavior. Verified the fix actually renders correctly despite the Vite block still applying to any real rebuild: patched the already-built, gitignored `dist/assets/*.css` with the identical rule (restored byte-for-byte afterward), reloaded through the same static-serve+Edge pipeline, and screenshotted the exact pixels — the clipped text and chip border now visibly soften into the background instead of hard-cutting, confirmed by eye at 4x zoom on the literal edge in question, not inferred from a diff. **Last updated: 2026-07-27 (Cycle 195 — found and fixed a real, previously-unnoticed second choke point for the exact same class of bug Cycles 192-193 fixed. While re-reading `SmartIntakeService.createVerticalSlice` to double-check a Cycle 194 claim, noticed it unconditionally overrides `timeline` (not `flags`/`vitals`) before calling `createPatient` — a doubly-confirming detail for Cycle 194's "no fix needed" conclusion, since that endpoint's own malformed timeline never even reaches `createPatient` to begin with. Following that thread to `EmergencyPatientService.updatePatient` (`backend/src/modules/emergency-os/emergency-os.services.ts:1089`) found it does a completely raw `{...current, ...patch, id}` merge with zero normalization — none of Cycle 192/193's `normalizePatientFlags()`/`normalizePatientVitals()` guards apply here at all. Traced this to a real, live, currently-reachable production endpoint: `PATCH /api/patients/:patientId` (`PlatformSystemsController.updateEmergencyPatient`), authenticated (`AuthGuard('jwt')`) and permission-gated (`READ_PHI`/`WRITE_PHI`) but with a fully unvalidated `Record<string, any>` body — any caller with write access can PATCH `flags` as `{type,...}` objects or `vitals` as `bpSystolic`/`bpDiastolic`, silently reintroducing both bugs already fixed for the create path. Confirmed the frontend's own client wrapper for this endpoint (`updateEmergencyPatientRecord` in `patientManagementApi.ts`) is currently unused — every real frontend `updatePatient` call goes to the local Zustand store, not this HTTP endpoint — so no live frontend regression exists today, but the backend API surface itself was genuinely unguarded regardless of whether today's frontend happens to exercise it. Checked all 3 other real internal callers of `updatePatient` before fixing (a reception-escalation flags-append that already sends plain strings, an OCR-intake demographics-only patch, and one test patching only `roomId`) to confirm the fix couldn't regress any of them. Fixed by applying the same `normalizePatientFlags()`/`normalizePatientVitals()` normalization inside `updatePatient` itself, conditionally on `patch.flags`/`patch.vitals` being present so an omitted field is never coerced into an empty array. New dedicated spec, `emergency-patient-update-normalization.spec.ts` (6 tests: object-shaped flags patched in get normalized identically to creation-time; a plain-string flags patch — the real `ReceptionWorkspaceService.escalate` shape — passes through unchanged; `bpSystolic`/`bpDiastolic` in a patched vitals entry map to `sbp`/`dbp`; `recordedBy` defaults to the patient's own `assignedStaffId`; a patch that omits `flags`/`vitals` entirely leaves both completely untouched; an explicitly-empty `flags: []` patch genuinely clears flags rather than being treated as "no patch given"). Verified: new suite 1/1 (6/6) plus the pre-existing `updatePatient`-touching specs re-run clean; backend `tsc`/`eslint` both 0 errors (3 pure-formatting `prettier/prettier` findings in the new spec, fixed via `eslint --fix`, re-verified passing after); full backend suite **259/259 suites, 2183/2183 tests** (was 258/258, 2177/2177 — +1/+6, zero regressions; one run hit the same pre-existing CPU-contention-flaky `encryption.service.spec.ts` timing test documented since Cycle 181, re-confirmed via a clean isolated run and a second clean full-suite run, unrelated to this change). See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-27 (Cycle 194 — closed out the last unexamined thread from Cycle 191's field-mapping evidence map: the `timeline`/`JourneyEvent` shape divergence, where `SmartIntake.tsx`'s vertical-slice flow sends timeline entries shaped `{id, patientId, type, timestamp, summary, metadata, from?, to?}` with no `staffId` at all, against the canonical `{id, from?, to, timestamp, staffId, note?}` — and `EmergencyPatientService.createPatient`'s `timeline: normalized.timeline || [...]` line stores whatever it's given completely verbatim, exactly the same unguarded pattern that caused the real `flags` (Cycle 192) and `vitals` (Cycle 193) bugs. Traced every real consumer before concluding anything, the same rigor those two fixes used: the one true UI-facing reader of backend-sourced journey events, `src/utils/patientTimeline.ts`'s `mapJourneyEvent`, already reads `eventRecord.staffId || eventRecord.actorStaffId || eventRecord.by || ''` (an optional field feeding an optional `actor?` display slot, confirmed via `staffLabel()` returning `undefined` cleanly on an empty id — no crash, no misleading value) and already categorizes by `eventRecord.type`/`from`/`to`, fields the vertical-slice's entries actually do send; backend's own `flow-snapshots.ts` only reads `event.type` (present) for a follow-up-scheduling check; `emergency-os.services.ts`'s summary-line fallback (`patient.timeline?.[...]?.note || patient.chiefComplaint`) already degrades gracefully when `note` is absent (the vertical-slice sends `summary`, not `note`); and the one place that reads `event.staffId` without a fallback, `emergencyStore.ts`'s `workflowLogFromJourneyEvent`, only ever operates on the frontend store's own natively-created `JourneyEvent`s (always populated with `staffId`) — never on the raw backend `getJourney()` payload, which flows through a completely separate, already-defensive code path. Unlike `flags`/`vitals`, this divergence was already fully absorbed by defensive code written for exactly this shape — confirmed with evidence, not assumed, closing the thread with a "no fix needed" finding rather than leaving it open or patching something that isn't broken. **This closes roadmap item #18's evidence-map triage completely** — all 4 field-shape questions Cycle 191 raised now have a resolved, evidence-backed answer (`notes`: no fix needed, Cycle 193; `flags`: fixed, Cycle 192; `vitals`: fixed, Cycle 193; `timeline`: no fix needed, this cycle). **Last updated: 2026-07-25 (Cycle 193 — continued acting on Cycle 191's field-mapping evidence, fixing a second and even more severe real bug from the same source: `SmartIntake.tsx`'s vertical-slice flow sends blood pressure as `bpSystolic`/`bpDiastolic` rather than the canonical `EmergencyVitals.sbp`/`.dbp`, and omits the required `recordedBy` field entirely. Confirmed real impact before fixing, not assumed: grepped both trees for `.sbp`/`.dbp` consumers and found BP is one of the most widely-read vitals fields in the app — `qSOFA.tsx` (sepsis screening's hypotension criterion), `alertEngine.ts` (critical SBP<90/>180 alerts), `reassessmentEngine.ts` (SBP-triggered reassessment), `clinical-protocol.service.ts` and `deterioration-prediction-v3.service.ts` on the backend, plus the primary BP display in `PatientDetailPanel.tsx` and half a dozen other summary/handoff surfaces. For a patient created through the same Smart Intake vertical-slice path as Cycle 192's flags bug, blood pressure would silently read as missing everywhere — showing as `--` in the UI and never triggering hypotension-driven alerts, sepsis-screen criteria, or reassessment logic, regardless of how low the real value was. Fixed at the same canonical choke point, `EmergencyPatientService.createPatient`, with `normalizeVitalsEntry()`/`normalizePatientVitals()`: maps `bpSystolic`→`sbp`/`bpDiastolic`→`dbp` when the canonical field is absent (canonical wins if both are present), defaults `recordedBy` to the assigned staff id or `'intake'`, and correctly treats a `null` BP value (an empty form field, not a real reading) as genuinely absent rather than storing `null` or `0` — verified this exact nullish-coalescing edge case with a standalone Node check before writing the test, not after. Also separately checked the third divergence Cycle 191 found (`notes` sent as `{id,type,body,authorId,createdAt,metadata}` vs. canonical `{id,text,authorId,timestamp}`) and found it needs no fix at all — nearly every real frontend consumer already reads `note.text || note.body`, a defensive fallback pattern already in place across a dozen+ files, closing that thread with confidence rather than "fixing" something that isn't actually broken. Wrote a new dedicated spec, `emergency-patient-vitals-normalization.spec.ts` (8 tests: canonical fields pass through, the real `bpSystolic`/`bpDiastolic` shape gets mapped, `recordedBy` defaulting through 2 fallback levels, the null-vs-absent edge case, single-object vs. array input, multi-entry independence, and empty-array preservation). Verified: new suite 1/1 (8/8), backend `tsc`/`eslint` both 0 errors, full backend suite 258/258 (2177/2177, +1 suite +8 tests, zero regressions). **Last updated: 2026-07-25 (Cycle 192 — found and fixed a real bug that Cycle 191's field-mapping investigation surfaced but didn't act on. Tracing `SmartIntake.tsx`'s vertical-slice flow's `flags` field revealed it sends structured objects (`{type, reason, severity, detectedAt}`) rather than the canonical `EmergencyPatient.flags: string[]` — and `EmergencyPatientService.createPatient` stored `input.flags` completely as-given, with zero type coercion (`flags: normalized.flags || (priority-based default)`). Confirmed real, concrete impact rather than treating it as a type-level nitpick: grepped both trees for `.flags.includes(...)`/`.flags.some(...)` usage and found it's load-bearing in over a dozen places — 5+ backend call sites (reassessment-queue filtering, EMS-high-risk detection, admission-pending checks, escalation logic) and 6+ frontend call sites (`NEWS2.tsx`/`qSOFA.tsx`'s clinical-score reassessment triggers, `HandoffBriefGenerator.tsx`, `calculatorSave.ts`), every one written assuming string entries. For a patient created via Smart Intake's vertical-slice "Create-and-triage" action with P1/P2 priority or a reassessment-triggering vital sign — exactly the highest-acuity patients — `patient.flags` would silently contain unmatchable objects instead of `'HighRisk'`/`'ReassessmentDue'` strings, meaning every one of those `.includes()` checks would quietly return false. Fixed at the single canonical choke point, `EmergencyPatientService.createPatient`, with a new `normalizePatientFlags()` helper that accepts strings as-is, extracts `.type` from object-shaped entries (verified to exactly match `PatientFlag` enum values), drops unrecognizable entries instead of storing garbage, and dedupes — so every caller is protected regardless of what shape it sends, not just the one that triggered the discovery. Wrote a new dedicated spec, `emergency-patient-flags-normalization.spec.ts` (7 tests: plain strings pass through, the real object shape gets normalized, a string+object mix is handled, unrecognizable entries are dropped rather than stored, cross-shape deduplication, and 2 tests pinning the pre-existing P1/P2-default and empty-array-stays-empty edge cases so the fix provably doesn't change behavior it shouldn't). Verified: new suite 1/1 (7/7), backend `tsc`/`eslint` both 0 errors, full backend suite 257/257 (2169/2169, +1 suite +7 tests, zero regressions). Deliberately backend-only — the frontend side of this bug (its own local pre-persist state) wasn't touched, since verifying a frontend change is still blocked by the persistent `esbuild` sandbox issue; the backend fix closes the real data-integrity risk at its actual boundary regardless. See Cycle log below).**

**Last updated: 2026-07-25 (Cycle 190 — fresh dependency/security re-audit, first since Cycle 182 (8 cycles ago). Both trees now show a much larger "high" count than last measured — frontend 26 (was 0), backend 38 — but investigation traced almost all of it to one root cause, not a new class of real risk: an old `brace-expansion`/`minimatch` DoS advisory (GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg, `<=5.0.7` vulnerable) that several dev-only tooling packages pin internally and haven't released a fix for. Traced three separate un-fixable chains with hard evidence, not assumption: (1) `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y@6.10.2` — the actual latest published version — still declares `minimatch: ^3.1.2`, whose own newest release (`3.1.5`) requires `brace-expansion: ^1.1.7`, whose newest 1.x release (`1.1.16`) is *still* inside the vulnerable range — there is no compatible patched release anywhere in that chain, confirmed via `npm view`, not guessed; (2) `jest`'s internal `@jest/reporters`/`babel-plugin-istanbul` chain pins an old `glob@7.2.3`, and `npm audit`'s own suggested "fix" (`jest@25.0.0`) is actually a downgrade from the installed `29.7.0`/`30.4.2` — verified by checking real installed versions, not trusted at face value (this program's Cycle 182 lesson applied again); (3) backend-only: `firebase-admin`'s `google-gax` and `mongoose`'s `gcp-metadata`→`gaxios` chains each pin their own internal `rimraf@5.0.10`→`glob@10.5.0`, invisible to and unfixable by any change to our own direct dependencies. One real, verified, if audit-count-invisible improvement made: bumped backend's own direct `rimraf` 5.0.5→6.1.3 (a genuine forward upgrade, not a downgrade — confirmed via `npm view` version history), cleaning our own `prebuild` script's dependency chain to a modern `glob@13.0.6`; **this does not reduce `npm audit`'s reported count**, since the identical advisory persists via the two third-party-pinned chains above that a change to our own `package.json` cannot reach — stated plainly rather than left to look like more progress than it is. Verified safe: backend `tsc`/`eslint` 0 errors, a working-CLI smoke test, and the full suite (2 of 3 runs 256/256 clean, 1 run's single failure isolated to the same pre-existing CPU-contention-sensitive timing test documented since Cycle 181 — re-confirmed via a 9ms isolated run, nowhere near its 100ms threshold, and fully unrelated to rimraf). Re-confirmed items #5 (`sharp`/`@xenova/transformers` — same conclusion, fresh 2026 CVE IDs) and #19 (`react-router` — unchanged, still needs the 7.x migration) with current evidence rather than carried-forward assumption. Opened new roadmap item #21 documenting the full un-fixable `brace-expansion` chain finding. See Cycle log below).**

| Backend dependency audit | `npm audit --omit=dev` (in `backend/`) | **8 vulnerabilities** (1 moderate, 6 high, **1 critical**) — regressed from the last-recorded "4 known findings, unchanged." Critical + most highs trace to `protobufjs` (arbitrary code execution, multiple CVEs) pulled in transitively via `@xenova/transformers` → `onnxruntime-web` → `onnx-proto`; also `sharp` (high, libvips CVEs), `dd-trace` (high, W3C baggage header DoS), `brace-expansion` (high, DoS). `npm audit fix` clears the non-breaking ones; the `@xenova/transformers` chain needs `--force` (breaking downgrade) — this is the same abandoned-package migration already tracked as roadmap item #12 below, now materially more urgent given the critical severity |
| Frontend test suite | `npm run test:run` (vitest) | see below |
| Backend test suite | `npm test` (jest, in `backend/`) | see below |

**Not fixed this cycle:** the two dependency-audit regressions above are reported, not remediated — `npm audit fix --force` on the backend is a breaking dependency change (downgrades `@xenova/transformers`) and deserves its own verified pass, not a drive-by inside a file-restructuring cycle. Flagged as the new top of the roadmap below.

*(Test suite result rows below were filled in after both suites finished — see "Testing, accessibility & delivery" gauge.)*

## Cycle 160 — dependency-audit regression, closed (mostly)

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Frontend `npm audit --omit=dev` | **0 vulnerabilities** (was 2) — `npm audit fix` cleanly bumped `axios`, `protobufjs`, `js-yaml`, `shell-quote`, `brace-expansion` within their existing `package.json` semver ranges |
| Backend `npm audit --omit=dev` | **2 high** (was 8: 1 moderate, 6 high, 1 critical) — `npm audit fix` (non-force) cleared `typeorm`, `brace-expansion`, `dd-trace` and dependents; a targeted `overrides` entry (`protobufjs: ^7.6.5`, package.json) deduped the last critical finding (arbitrary code execution via `onnx-proto`'s vendored `protobufjs@6.11.6`) onto the same 7.6.5 already used elsewhere in this tree via `firebase-admin`/`google-gax` — zero new packages introduced |
| Real embedding smoke test | **Verified** — `pipeline('feature-extraction', 'Xenova/all-mpnet-base-v2')` against real cached model weights, real text in, real 768-dim float vector out, after the `protobufjs` override. This is the actual runtime path the critical finding sat on (`@xenova/transformers` → `onnxruntime-web` → `onnx-proto` → `protobufjs`), not just a clean `npm ls` |
| Backend test suite | 245/245 suites, 1922/1922 tests — unchanged, re-run twice across the dependency changes |
| Frontend test suite | **Not re-verified this cycle** — see below |

**Still open: `sharp <0.35.0` (high, libvips CVEs) via `@xenova/transformers`'s vendored copy.** Attempted the same override approach; the fix requires npm to write a fresh native `.node` binary, which a local Windows Application Control policy in this dev sandbox blocked outright (`ERR_DLOPEN_FAILED`) — confirmed via direct `dlopen()` test, not just the wrapped error. Reverted to the original, already-trusted `sharp@0.32.6` rather than ship an unverified native-dependency bump; `sharp` is 0.x (pre-1.0 semver, minor bumps can be breaking) and is eagerly imported by `@xenova/transformers`'s pipeline machinery even for text-only feature-extraction — a broken sharp would take the whole embedding path down, so this isn't a low-stakes swap to make blind. Left open for a cycle with CI/Linux verification access (production runs `FROM node:20-alpine`, a different binary entirely, so this sandbox's block has no bearing on production safety — it's a *local verification* blocker, not a production risk indicator either way).

**Frontend runtime verification also blocked this cycle, unrelated to the dependency changes' correctness:** after `npm audit fix` touched `node_modules`, the same Application Control policy re-flagged the `esbuild` native binary Vite/Vitest depend on to even load their config file — `tsc` and `eslint` don't spawn it and both stayed clean, but `npm run test:run` cannot run in this sandbox for the remainder of this session. Reported honestly rather than assumed passing.

## Cycle 161 — real `strictNullChecks` blast-radius baseline established (166 errors), 44 closed

The `esbuild` Application Control block from Cycle 160 is still in effect in this sandbox (confirmed by re-testing `npm run test:run` before starting — same `spawn UNKNOWN`), so this cycle was scoped to backend-only work fully verifiable via `tsc`/`jest` (frontend `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` re-confirmed clean, 0 errors, as a sanity check that they're unaffected).

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `strictNullChecks` blast radius (`tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.eslint.json --strictNullChecks true`) | **166 errors → 122 errors** (44 closed) across the ~800-file backend tree — the roadmap's "blast radius unknown" is now a real, categorized number |
| Backend `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.eslint.json` (real project config, full scope incl. tests) | **0 errors**, unchanged |
| Frontend `tsc --noEmit` | **0 errors**, unchanged |
| Backend full jest suite | **245/245 suites, 1922/1922 tests** — including `test/operational-intelligence-build-snapshot.spec.ts`, which directly exercises the most heavily-touched file this cycle |
| Backend `npm run lint` | **21 pre-existing errors** (all `prettier/prettier` formatting, in 7 files this cycle didn't touch: CIG projection/entity mapper files, `intent-classifier.service.ts`, `document-chunker.spec.ts`) — see callout below |

**What the 44 closed errors actually were:** re-ran `tsc` with `--strictNullChecks true` against the full-scope config to get a real count instead of the roadmap's "unknown," then categorized all 166 by TS error code: 41 were `TS2345`/`TS2339` errors bearing the exact text `type 'never'`, all traced to the same single root cause — `const arr = [];` accumulator arrays with no type annotation, which TypeScript infers as `any[]` when `strictNullChecks` is off (today's setting) but as `never[]` when it's on, breaking every later `.push()`/property-access. This pattern repeated across 10 files (`lib/operational-intelligence/buildSnapshot.ts` alone had 15 of the 41, all from two untyped arrays feeding the exported `anomalies`/`recommendations` fields — fixed by annotating them against the already-existing `OperationalIntelligenceSnapshotOutput` contract type instead of duplicating it). Fixed all 10 files with explicit array element types, mostly reusing an existing declared return type, entity type, or sibling `Awaited<ReturnType<...>>` idiom already present in the same file rather than inventing new shapes.

**Bonus find while fixing `tenant-provisioning.service.ts`:** fixing its one `never[]` error surfaced 3 more, different errors nearby — `resolveWorkspaceDefaults()`'s `.filter()` used a type predicate reading `workspace is ReturnType<...normalizeWorkspaceDefault>`, but `normalizeWorkspaceDefault` can return `null`, so the predicate never actually narrowed `null` out of the *type* (even though the runtime `.filter(Boolean)` genuinely does drop falsy values — this was a type-annotation-only bug, not a runtime one). The correct idiom, `NonNullable<ReturnType<...>>`, was already used correctly two methods away in the same file (line 259) — this one call site just didn't match it. Fixed to match.

**Real, verified regression check:** frontend and backend `tsc` both re-confirmed 0 errors under their actual project configs (not just the experimental `--strictNullChecks true` flag), and the full backend jest suite re-ran clean at 245/245 (1922/1922) after all 10 files changed — including the one spec file that directly exercises the most-touched file (`buildSnapshot.ts`).

**What this cycle deliberately did NOT do:** flip `strictNullChecks: true` in `backend/tsconfig.json`. 122 errors remain, and unlike the 44 closed here, most of the rest are genuine "possibly null/undefined" findings (`TS18048`/`TS18047`/`TS2531`/`TS2532`, ~40 of the 122) inside sensitive, high-traffic services — `auth.service.ts` (11), `subscriptions.service.ts` (10), `ai.service.ts` (9), `chat.service.ts` (6 more, a different cluster than the ones fixed here) — where the correct fix requires understanding each call site's real nullability invariant, not a mechanical annotation. Rushing that without the ability to cross-check against a live frontend integration run (blocked this session) would risk exactly the kind of drive-by mistake this project's cycle discipline exists to avoid. Left as a well-scoped, now-quantified P2 roadmap item instead of a global flag flip.

**Also found, not fixed:** backend `npm run lint` currently has 21 real, pre-existing `prettier/prettier` errors in 7 files, none touched by this cycle and none touched recently by this session's own history (last commit on any of them: `14233485`, 2026-07-21, CIG/AI work). This directly contradicts this file's own "What's genuinely solid" claim that backend `eslint` was clean — that claim's "Cycle 158" citation, on closer reading, only ever re-confirmed *frontend* lint fresh; backend lint had not actually been re-run this session until now. Not fixed here (out of this cycle's chosen scope, and touching files with recent unrelated activity deserves its own pass rather than a drive-by inside a type-safety cycle) — corrected in "What's genuinely solid" below and added as a fresh roadmap item.

Also re-verified and corrected a stale roadmap claim: the ported Mongo integration suite's real blocker is the same `esbuild` Application Control issue affecting all Vitest work, not a Mongo-specific one.
- **(Cycle 167)** `strictNullChecks` blast radius down to 89. Closed `intent-classifier.service.ts` (one utility function's `T | null` return meeting an optional-property `T | undefined` field, fixed once at the single call site) and `device-token.service.ts` (entity fields typed non-optional despite being DB-nullable — widened to match, the idiomatic TypeORM `?` convention, zero behavior change). Found a real, previously-unflagged gap along the way: `DeviceTokenService` has no dedicated test spec at all.
- **(Cycle 168)** `strictNullChecks` blast radius down to 82. - **(Cycle 169)** `strictNullChecks` blast radius down to 71. The Cycle 166 pattern recurred a 3rd time (3 memory services), plus a new variant in `memory-fabric.service.ts` (`?? undefined` boundary conversion). Found a genuinely different bug shape in `integration-hub.service.ts`: TypeORM's `FindOptionsWhere<T>` rejects bare `null` for nullable columns even when the entity itself is correctly typed — the fix (TypeORM's `IsNull()` operator) has real behavioral stakes in a WHERE clause (unlike `create()`, `null` and `undefined` mean different things there), so verified it against a real SQLite database rather than just reasoning about equivalence.
- **(Cycle 170)** `strictNullChecks` blast radius down to 61. Found and fixed a genuine functional bug in `asset-access.service.ts`: role-recommended assets were computed from a property name (`recommendedAssetIds`) that doesn't exist on the `RoleProfile` entity (the real field is `preferredAssetIds`), so every user's role-based asset recommendations were silently empty. Widened 2 security-critical entities (`BiometricConfig.lockedUntil`, `TwoFactor.secret`/`.backupCodes`) to their real DB-nullable types, and narrowed `BiometricService.verifyBiometric`'s return type to match its actual all-succeed-or-throw control flow. - **(Cycle 172)** `strictNullChecks` blast radius down to 37 — the entire remaining tail of scattered small clusters closed in one pass across 10 files. Found a real webhook-security gap in `subscriptions.controller.ts`: Stripe webhook handling had no guard against a missing raw request body or a missing webhook secret, both required for HMAC signature verification, and would have thrown an uncontrolled runtime error instead of failing closed with a clear rejection. Widened `AuditLog.userId` for its deliberate HIPAA-anonymization write path (verified the tamper-detection hash function already normalized the value, so the fix is provably behavior-neutral). Everything left in the roadmap item is now exactly the 4 large, previously-deferred sensitive clusters.
- **(Cycle 173)** `strictNullChecks` blast radius down to 25 — closed the first of the 4 large deferred clusters, `subscriptions.service.ts`. Found the sibling gap to Cycle 172's webhook fix: `createCheckoutSession` and `createCustomerPortalSession` called Stripe's API unconditionally, with no guard against the client being unconfigured, meaning any environment without Stripe set up would throw an uncontrolled runtime error on the very first checkout attempt instead of a clear, typed rejection. Fixed with a `ServiceUnavailableException` guard — and added 2 new tests specifically to exercise it, since the existing spec force-injects a mock Stripe client after construction, meaning this failure path had never actually been run by any test before.
- **(Cycle 174)** `strictNullChecks` blast radius down to 20 — closed `smart-handover-v2.service.ts` (real SBAR clinical handover summary generation). Fixed with one new type (`HydratedPatientContext`) capturing what the function actually promises, rather than 5 separate defensive coalesces — verified against the one real, unmocked test exercising this exact code path. - **(Cycle 186)** Closed `SentinelController` on roadmap item #17 — the last remaining candidate, finally tackled now that it no longer had to compete with a smaller, better-scoped target.
