- **(Cycle 187)** With item #17 fully closed, picked up roadmap item #4 (`ts-prune` unused-export triage), which had sat untouched at "investigation-only" for many cycles — likely because a flat "~2,655 combined findings" reads as too unbounded for a single well-scoped pass. Tried the two blocked candidates first and ruled them out with real evidence rather than skipping silently: item #8 needs a fresh production build to re-verify, but `vite build` still fails on the same `esbuild`/Application Control `spawn UNKNOWN` this program has documented as blocking all Vite config-loading since Cycle 160; item #6 needs Docker, and `docker`/`docker ps` both report command-not-found in this sandbox. `ts-prune` itself needs neither — pure static analysis via the TS compiler API — so it stayed viable.

  Ran fresh `ts-prune` against both tsconfigs rather than trusting the stale roadmap estimate, then built real methodology instead of eyeballing the list: split the backend's 1350 raw findings into 728 actually inside `backend/` vs. 622 in shared repo-root files (`types/`, `engine/`, `ml-services/shared/`) that had been miscounted into the original "~702 backend" figure; split those 728 into 328 "used in module" (ts-prune's weak-signal tag — the export IS referenced, just not through the exact binding it's checking, not real dead code) vs. 400 true zero-reference. Grouped the 400 by file rather than reading them one at a time, which surfaced the dominant shape immediately: 19 files account for 274 of the 400 (68%), and every one of those 19 is a module-level `index.ts` barrel (`modules/moe-router`, `modules/tool-calling`, `modules/sentinel`, `modules/ai/foundation`, etc.). Checked real importers for each — `grep` for anything importing the bare module path, not assumption — and found most of these barrels genuinely are imported by other modules (`moe-router`, `surveillance`, `platform-governance`, `simulation`, `hospital-map`, `telemetry` all have real consumers reaching them through the bare path), so ts-prune is just flagging individual named exports within an otherwise-live barrel as a false positive. One barrel, `backend/src/index.ts` (a single line, `export * from './services/service-registry'`), had **zero importers anywhere** in the tree — confirmed via repo-wide grep, not inferred from the pattern — while its underlying `service-registry.ts` exports remain genuinely reachable through `main.ts`, `health.routes.ts`, and `services/index.ts`, none of which go through this particular barrel. Deleted it outright.

  Frontend `ts-prune` was also run fresh (4356 findings, vs. the stale "~1,953" estimate — real ~2.2x growth over the cycles since that number was last measured) but deliberately not triaged this cycle — same "don't overreach" discipline as every prior cycle that found a bigger-than-one-cycle scope and said so honestly rather than rushing a shaky pass.

  **Verification:** backend `tsc`/`eslint` both 0 errors; full backend suite unchanged at 256/256 suites, 2162/2162 tests (the deleted file had no test of its own, since nothing imported it to test); re-ran `ts-prune` after the deletion and confirmed the count dropped by exactly the expected 36 (1350→1314), isolating the fix with no side effects. Committed and pushed.

  **Lesson: when a roadmap item's number looks too large to triage in one pass, the fix usually isn't to attempt a fraction of it at random — it's to build real categorization first (location, signal strength, file-level clustering) and let the data show where the leverage actually is. Here, grouping by file turned "400 scattered findings" into "19 files explain 68% of them," which is a fundamentally different, much more tractable problem than the flat list ever looked like.**
- **(Cycle 188)** Finished the module-barrel verification Cycle 187 had explicitly left open ("spot-checked, not individually verified") for 7 of the ~19 barrel files identified as the dominant `ts-prune` false-positive shape. Used a more rigorous repo-wide import search this time — Cycle 187's narrower pattern had a real blind spot, missing `ai/foundation`'s genuine importer (`ai.module.ts`) — caught and corrected before concluding anything, rather than trusting the earlier quick check. The other 6 (`tool-calling`, `cost-optimizer`, `evaluation`, `training`, `memory`, `sentinel`) confirmed fully orphaned: `app.module.ts` wires each in by its specific `.module.ts` file, never the bare barrel path, and nothing else in the repo — checked frontend too — imports the barrel either. Read all 6 files first to confirm they're pure `export *` re-export barrels with no side effects before deleting any of them. Diffed the full `ts-prune` output before/after rather than trusting the raw count alone: 71 findings removed as expected, but 5 new weak-signal findings appeared — investigated rather than dismissed, and fully explained (types previously satisfied by the barrel's own re-export, now correctly re-classified as used-only-locally now that the barrel's gone; still not real dead code). Net -66 (1314→1248). Zero regressions (256/256 suites, 2162/2162 tests, unchanged — none of the 6 had test coverage of their own). **7 barrel files removed across Cy187-188, 107 findings resolved total; the barrel-file sub-investigation is now closed, though item #4 as a whole remains open.**
- **(Cycle 189)** Checked backend's last big remaining `ts-prune` cluster, `services/index.ts` (55 findings) — confirmed genuinely live (10 real importers among the legacy `api/*.routes.ts` files) — closing out the backend barrel-hunting vein Cy187-188 opened: everything left there now needs individual per-export judgment, not whole-file deletion. Extended the same barrel methodology to frontend for the first time, this time explicitly accounting for this repo's real path aliases (`@/*` → `./src/*`) so the importer search wouldn't have a blind spot the way Cycle 187's first pass briefly did. Result was a genuinely different, more interesting shape than backend's: most barrel candidates were live, one (`contracts/index.ts`, 72 findings) turned out to be a deliberately-unadopted-yet migration bridge (its own header comment says so — "New code should import from `@/contracts`... over time, migrate imports"), correctly left alone rather than deleted just because it matched the zero-importer pattern. One more (`services/native/index.ts`, 16 findings) led somewhere real: reading its content instead of just trusting the "zero importers" signal revealed a complete, working WebAuthn biometric helper — not dead code by any reasonable definition. Pulled the thread with `git log --diff-filter=D` rather than guessing, and found the actual story: commit `ce80849c` ("open-access demo... auth UI removal") deliberately deleted the one page that used to call this whole `native/` layer, as part of a real product pivot — not a bug, but it left the entire client-side biometric integration, and the backend's fully-built, fully-tested `BiometricService`/`BiometricController` (25 tests from Cycle 178) alongside it, with no way for a user to ever reach them. Opened as roadmap item #20 rather than either deleting 446 lines of real auth/crypto code unilaterally or trying to rebuild a login page without any product sign-off on which direction is even wanted. Deleted the one unambiguous leftover from that same commit — `src/pages/BiometricSetup.css`, a stylesheet with zero references anywhere. Verified: frontend `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both 0 errors; full Jest/Vitest run still blocked by the same persistent `esbuild` sandbox issue as every prior cycle, disclosed explicitly rather than glossed over — mitigated for this specific change since a CSS file deletion has no import-graph risk beyond what `tsc` and a repo-wide grep already confirmed. No backend files touched this cycle.
- **(Cycle 190)** First dependency/security re-audit since Cycle 182 (8 cycles ago) — the headline numbers looked alarming at first (frontend 0→26, backend recount to 38 high) until traced to source rather than reacted to. Grouped every finding by its `via` chain and found nearly all of it converges on one shared advisory, `brace-expansion`'s DoS vulnerability (GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg), reached through 3 separate, independently-confirmed-unfixable paths. Applied Cycle 182's "check real installed versions before trusting `fixAvailable`" lesson rigorously: `npm audit` suggested `jest@25.0.0` as the fix — checked the real installed version first (`29.7.0`/`30.4.2`) and confirmed that's a downgrade, not a fix. Same discipline for `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`: used `npm view` to confirm its actual latest release (6.10.2, already installed) still declares `minimatch: ^3.1.2`, and that minimatch line's own newest release still requires a `brace-expansion` version inside the vulnerable range — a definitive "no fix exists," not a guess.

  Found one of the three suggested fixes was, unlike the others, a genuine forward version: `rimraf` 5.0.5→6.1.3. Checked its real usage first (`prebuild: rimraf dist`, about as low-risk as a CLI dependency gets) and applied it. `npm install` showed real package churn but a full before/after audit diff showed **zero change** in the vulnerability count — traced why rather than left confused: `firebase-admin`'s `google-gax` and `mongoose`'s `gcp-metadata`→`gaxios` both pin their own internal `rimraf@5.0.10`→`glob@10.5.0`, completely independent of this repo's own declaration. Kept the bump as a real, verified improvement, but wrote it up explicitly as not reducing the count rather than letting the diff imply more than it delivered.

  One test failure surfaced across 3 full-suite verification runs: `encryption.service.spec.ts`'s hash-throughput timing assertion, the same test flagged as CPU-contention-flaky in Cycle 181. Re-applied that cycle's exact recipe: isolated run measured 9ms (nowhere near the 100ms threshold, unrelated to `rimraf`), and a follow-up full run came back clean (256/256, 2162/2162) — 2 of 3 runs clean, the one failure fully explained as pre-existing noise, not a regression.

  Refreshed items #5 and #19 with today's evidence instead of letting old citations go stale — #5 now cites the actual current CVE IDs behind the same unchanged conclusion; #19 reconfirmed the fix boundary is still `>=7.18.0`. Opened item #21 for the `brace-expansion` chain with the full 3-path evidence trail, explicitly noting low real-world exploitability (every affected package is dev/build tooling or an SDK's internal file-cleanup utility, never reached by untrusted input) rather than either alarmist framing or silent dismissal.
- **(Cycle 191)** Investigation-only cycle, no code changes. First closed out `ts-prune` barrel-hunting for good: checked the last 3 unverified candidates, the shared `lib/` barrels (`lib/cig`, `lib/native-ai`, `lib/patient-orchestration`, 177 combined findings) — all 3 confirmed heavily live across both trees via real importer checks, same rigor as Cycles 187-189. This closes the methodology out completely: every barrel-shaped candidate this program's `ts-prune` investigation ever surfaced has now been individually verified, not just pattern-matched, and only 7 files total across 4 cycles turned out to actually be dead.

  Then picked up roadmap item #18's validation-DTO sub-item, open since Cycle 180 specifically because building a safe whitelist DTO needed "enumerating every field real callers send today" first — a real prerequisite, not a stalling excuse. Traced all 5 real frontend callers of the 3 unvalidated intake endpoints down to their actual constructed payloads (not their TypeScript types, which are loosely-typed `any`/`Partial<Patient>` at the call-site level and wouldn't reveal what's really on the wire) and compared each against the canonical `EmergencyPatient`/`EmergencyVitals`/`JourneyEvent` types a naive DTO would otherwise be modeled on. The divergence was real and specific, not vague: `notes` has a consistent real shape (`{id,type,body,authorId,createdAt,metadata}`, used by 2 of 5 callers) that doesn't match the canonical interface's `{id,text,authorId,timestamp}` at all; `flags` is even more interesting — most callers send a plain `string[]` (matching canonical), but `SmartIntake.tsx`'s vertical-slice path sends structured objects (`{type,reason,severity,detectedAt}`) instead, a genuine inconsistency *between real callers*, not just against the idealized type; `vitals` uses `bpSystolic`/`bpDiastolic` field names where canonical declares `sbp`/`dbp` and requires a `recordedBy` field that no traced caller ever sends; and `timeline`/`JourneyEvent` turned out to have 3 distinct real shapes in simultaneous use, one of which (`SmartIntake.tsx`'s vertical-slice timeline entries) is missing the canonical-required `staffId` field entirely — exactly the kind of gap that would make a strict `required()` validator reject a real, working, currently-succeeding request. Also catalogued 7 fields real callers send that don't exist in the canonical interface at all (`phone`, `healthCardNumber`, `healthCard`, `source`, `lastAssessedTime`, `quickSafetyFlags`, `waitingRoomStatus`) — every one of these would be silently stripped or rejected under `forbidNonWhitelisted:true` today if a DTO were built straight from the canonical type without this evidence.
- **(Cycle 192)** Directly acted on a real finding Cycle 191 surfaced but hadn't fixed: `SmartIntake.tsx`'s vertical-slice flow builds `flags` as `{type, reason, severity, detectedAt}` objects, but `EmergencyPatientService.createPatient` stored `input.flags` verbatim with zero type coercion, and the canonical `EmergencyPatient.flags` type is `string[]`. Didn't stop at "this is a type mismatch" — grepped both trees for every real consumer of `.flags` and found it's load-bearing well beyond display code: 5+ backend call sites (reassessment-queue filtering, EMS-high-risk detection, admission-pending checks, escalation handling) and 6+ frontend call sites (`NEWS2.tsx`/`qSOFA.tsx`'s clinical-score reassessment triggers, `calculatorSave.ts`, `HandoffBriefGenerator.tsx`) all do `.flags.includes(PatientFlag.X)`, which silently returns `false` forever against an array of objects — no crash, no error, just a quietly-broken safety signal for exactly the highest-acuity patients (P1/P2 priority, or vitals that trigger reassessment) created through this one specific intake path.

  Fixed at the single canonical choke point (`EmergencyPatientService.createPatient`) rather than patching each consumer: a new `normalizePatientFlags()` helper passes strings through unchanged, extracts `.type` from object-shaped entries (verified the exact real shape against `SmartIntake.tsx`'s own `createFlag()` helper and confirmed `'HighRisk'`/`'ReassessmentDue'` match the `PatientFlag` enum's real values before writing the extraction logic), drops anything unrecognizable rather than storing `"[object Object]"` garbage, and dedupes. Every caller is protected now, not just the one that surfaced the bug. Wrote a dedicated new spec (`emergency-patient-flags-normalization.spec.ts`, 7 tests) covering the real object shape, a mixed string/object array, unrecognizable-entry dropping, cross-shape deduplication, and — importantly — 2 tests that pin the pre-existing P1/P2-default and empty-array-stays-empty behaviors unchanged, proving the fix is additive rather than a behavior change in disguise. Deliberately backend-only: the frontend's own local pre-persist state wasn't touched, since a frontend change still can't be verified in this sandbox (the persistent `esbuild` block) — the backend fix closes the actual data-integrity boundary regardless of what the frontend holds transiently before the API round-trip completes. Zero regressions (257/257 suites, 2169/2169 tests).
- **(Cycle 193)** Continued straight down Cycle 191's evidence trail: the `flags` bug fixed last cycle and the `vitals` bug fixed this cycle share the exact same source (`SmartIntake.tsx`'s vertical-slice flow) and the exact same fix location (`EmergencyPatientService.createPatient`), so checked the vitals divergence with the same rigor before assuming it mattered as much. It mattered more: grepped both trees for `.sbp`/`.dbp` consumers and found blood pressure is read far more widely than flags ever were — `qSOFA.tsx`'s sepsis-screening hypotension criterion, `alertEngine.ts`'s critical SBP<90/>180 alert, `reassessmentEngine.ts`'s SBP-triggered reassessment, 2 backend services (`clinical-protocol.service.ts`, `deterioration-prediction-v3.service.ts`), and the primary BP display across half a dozen patient-summary/handoff surfaces. `SmartIntake.tsx`'s `normalizeSmartIntakeVitals()` sends `bpSystolic`/`bpDiastolic`, never `sbp`/`dbp`, and never sends `recordedBy` at all (canonical type requires both `sbp`/`dbp` optionally and `recordedBy` as required) — meaning a real, currently-live pathway sends vitals that silently show as missing BP everywhere, for exactly the same class of high-acuity patients as Cycle 192's bug.
- **(Cycle 194)** Closed out item #18's evidence-map triage completely by resolving the 4th and last divergence Cycle 191 found: `SmartIntake.tsx`'s vertical-slice `timeline` entries are missing the canonical-required `staffId` field. Unlike the `flags`/`vitals` bugs, traced every real consumer and found this one causes no actual failure — the one UI-facing reader of backend journey events (`patientTimeline.ts`'s `mapJourneyEvent`) already falls back through `staffId`/`actorStaffId`/`by` into an optional display field, and the one non-defensive reader (`emergencyStore.ts`'s `workflowLogFromJourneyEvent`) only ever operates on the frontend store's own natively-created events, never the raw backend payload. Confirmed "no fix needed" with the same consumer-tracing rigor as a "yes, fix it" finding — investigation-only, zero code changes.
- **(Cycle 195)** Re-reading `createVerticalSlice` to double-check a Cycle 194 claim turned up a real second bug: `EmergencyPatientService.updatePatient` — the handler behind the live, authenticated `PATCH /api/patients/:patientId` endpoint — does a completely raw merge with none of Cycle 192/193's `flags`/`vitals` normalization, meaning the exact same 2 already-fixed bugs were fully reachable again through this second write path. Checked all 3 other real internal callers before fixing to confirm no regression (a reception-escalation flags-append already sending plain strings, an OCR-intake demographics-only patch, and a roomId-only test patch), then applied the same normalization inside `updatePatient` itself. 6 new tests, zero regressions (259/259 suites, 2183/2183 tests).
- **(Cycle 196)** First cycle to actually render and look at the app rather than rely on static analysis — served the existing `dist/` build statically and drove the real system Edge browser via Playwright to screenshot 6 pages, since `npm run dev` is still fully blocked by the `esbuild` sandbox issue (re-confirmed fresh: `vite.config.ts` fails to load with `Error: spawn UNKNOWN`). Found a real visual bug on the Whiteboard: the header's status-chip rail hard-clips its last chip mid-word when its content overflows the rail's width, with no visual cue it's scrollable — measured via DOM inspection (346px of content in a 288px rail), not guessed. Fixed with a theme-agnostic `mask-image` edge fade and verified the fix actually renders correctly by patching the equivalent rule directly into the already-built (gitignored) CSS bundle, reloading through the same screenshot pipeline, and confirming by eye at 4x zoom that the clipped text now fades instead of hard-cutting — a real visual-verification path for CSS-only changes despite the Vite rebuild block. Opened roadmap item #22 for this as an ongoing thread.
- **(Cycle 197)** Continued item #22, screenshotted 12 more pages, and found a substantially higher-impact bug than Cycle 196's: the Copilot launcher button rendered as an unstyled, full-viewport-height bar down the entire right edge of every page, instead of the small rounded pill its own CSS designs it as. Root-caused rather than patched around: its styling lived in a stylesheet that only loads as a side effect of lazily importing the full Copilot panel component, but the button specifically renders when that panel is closed — meaning the lazy import, and its CSS, had genuinely never fired on a fresh page load. Confirmed via live `getComputedStyle` (`position: static` instead of the intended `fixed`) and via the actual `dist/assets/` chunk layout, not guessed — this reproduces for every real user's first session, not just the static-serve verification method. Fixed by relocating the CSS to the app shell's own eagerly-loaded stylesheet; verified by hand-moving the same rule between the two real build chunks and confirming both a live screenshot and computed styles matched the intended pill design exactly (165×47px, `border-radius: 999px`, `position: fixed`, bottom-right corner) before trusting the source fix.
- **(Cycle 198)** Continued item #22, screenshotted 12 more pages (30 total across 3 cycles), and found a third real bug on the Clinical Calculator Hub: single-word category badges like "GENERAL" broke mid-letter across 2 lines ("GENERA"/"L") on cards with a long title. Diagnosed via computed styles rather than CSS-reading alone: the badge is a flex sibling of the title in a `display: flex` header, had no `flex-shrink: 0`, and got squeezed below its natural content width by a long title with nothing to absorb the pressure — with no space in a single word to wrap at, `overflow-wrap: break-word` split it mid-letter instead. Fixed with `flex-shrink: 0; white-space: nowrap` on the badge, redirecting the space pressure onto the title, which already wraps gracefully at word boundaries. Verified via the same dist-patch-and-screenshot technique, confirming all 4 visible badges on the page — including the 11-character "RESPIRATORY" — rendered correctly afterward, and via grep that this is the class's only definition anywhere (no competing rule).
- **(Cycle 199)** Continued item #22, screenshotted 14 more pages (44 total across 4 cycles), and found the highest-blast-radius bug of the thread: a large, solid dark-navy rectangle with no content, appearing on every command-center-style page. Traced via DOM inspection to `.cdl-zone--primary-actions`, a layout section wrapping the real "Critical actions" cards, whose entire background was being painted dark by an unrelated, deliberate app-wide sweep rule for `--primary` *button* modifiers — a plain substring-match selector (`[class*='--primary']`) that, having no word-boundary check, also matched this *zone's* class name purely by coincidence of substring. Confirmed via grep this was the only accidental victim among 15 real `--primary`-containing classes (the other 14 are genuine buttons the rule should catch), and separately checked its `[class*='cta']` sibling selector for the same collision risk, finding none. Fixed with a one-line `:not(.cdl-zone--primary-actions)` exclusion, following the exact pattern the rule already used for one previously-known false positive — the deliberate invariant itself untouched. Verified via the same dist-patch-and-screenshot pipeline: confirmed via `getComputedStyle` (background now fully transparent) and a live screenshot that the entire dark rectangle is gone.
- **(Cycle 200)** Continued item #22, screenshotted 14 more pages (58 total across 5 cycles), and found the thread's first content/copy bug rather than a layout one: the Hospital Maturity Assessment page's questions read "How mature is your hospital's ai maturity?" and "...iot readiness?" — both wrong-cased. Root cause: the question template blindly lowercases the whole dimension label to read grammatically mid-sentence, correct for 4 of 6 dimensions but destructive to the 2 whose labels contain acronyms ("AI", "IoT"). Fixed with an explicit, already-correctly-cased `midSentenceLabel` field per dimension rather than a smarter-but-fragile detection heuristic, since the set is small and fully enumerable. Being a JS/data change rather than CSS, couldn't use the dist-CSS-patch verification technique (a JS rebuild hits the same blocked Vite path) — instead verified mechanically with a standalone Node script computing all 6 question strings before/after, confirming byte-for-byte that only the 2 broken ones changed and the other 4 are identical to before.

  Fixed at the identical choke point with `normalizeVitalsEntry()`/`normalizePatientVitals()`: `sbp = raw.sbp ?? raw.bpSystolic` (canonical wins if both present), same for `dbp`, `recordedBy` defaults through `assignedStaffId` then a hardcoded `'intake'` fallback (matching the same fallback value the default timeline entry already used). One subtlety verified with a standalone Node script before writing the test, not assumed: `normalizeSmartIntakeVitals()`'s own `parseNumber()` helper returns `null` (not `undefined`) for an empty form field, and `raw.sbp ?? raw.bpSystolic` needed to correctly fall through a `null` `bpSystolic` too (nullish coalescing treats `null` and `undefined` identically) while still ending up `undefined` in the final output rather than a stored `null` — confirmed with 5 real input combinations run through the actual expression before trusting it.

  Separately, and just as important as the fix itself: went back to the third divergence Cycle 191 found (`notes` shape) rather than leaving it unresolved, and found it doesn't need fixing at all — grepped for `note.text`/`note.body` consumption and found the app already defensively reads `note.text || note.body` in more than a dozen files (`PatientDetailPanel.tsx`, `SepsisBundleTracker.tsx`, `StrokeCodeProtocol.tsx`, `shiftSummaryData.ts`, `patientAiContext.ts`, and others) — a real, existing, working accommodation for exactly the shape mismatch Cycle 191 flagged. Confirming a finding doesn't need action, with evidence, closes the thread as surely as fixing it would — and avoids the mistake of "fixing" code that already handles the real-world case correctly.

  8 new tests (`emergency-patient-vitals-normalization.spec.ts`) covering canonical pass-through, the real `bpSystolic`/`bpDiastolic` mapping, both `recordedBy` fallback levels, the null-vs-absent edge case, single-object vs. array input (the pre-fix code's own dual-mode handling), independent multi-entry normalization, and empty-array preservation. Zero regressions (258/258 suites, 2177/2177 tests).
- **(Cycle 201)** First real, unmocked full-stack round trip this program has ever run for the AI chat feature — real compiled backend + real compiled frontend + a real browser — found and fixed a total, unconditional break: every real call to `POST /api/ai/node/conversational` had been failing 400 "property stream should not exist" since 2026-06-13 (~120+ cycles), invisible to every prior test because none exercised a real `class-validator` pass against this DTO (the one existing spec asserted against source text). Fixed by declaring the field; added a genuine regression test confirmed red-without/green-with the fix. Also re-ran the pre-existing `interactive-ai.spec.mjs` suite (IX12-14) fresh for the first time since 2026-07-18 — 8/8 clean. Zero regressions (260/260 suites, 2186/2186 tests, +1 suite +3 tests).
- **(Cycle 203)** Found and fixed a real bug in `/notifications` via a same-condition side-by-side comparison against `/security`/`/audit`: a raw browser `SyntaxError` was leaking directly into the UI on a failed API call instead of the clean, designed fallback message the app's own shared `parseApiResponse` helper already provides — 4 methods in `NotificationService.ts` called raw `response.json()` instead of the file's own already-correct pattern used by its other 4 sibling methods. Also found the real service had zero real test coverage (the same-named test file tests an unrelated fixture; the component test mocks the service wholesale) and added a dedicated suite mocking only the actual network boundary, closing the exact gap that let this bug hide.
- **(Cycle 204)** Found and fixed a real bug affecting 2 separate pages from a single root cause: `ClinicalAlertsPage` and `HospitalCommandCenter` both passed their actual page content to the shared `EmergencyRoutePage` wrapper via a prop (`activeWork`) the component never declared — only `children` — so it was silently dropped every time, with the surrounding summary cards (fed by a different, correctly-wired prop) rendering fine enough to mask it. Fixed at the component level (`activeWork ?? children`), restoring both pages with zero changes to either page's own source; added a 3-test regression suite plus a standalone logic proof since Vitest is still blocked.
- **(Cycle 205)** Found and fixed a real CSS Grid bug affecting `/competencies` and `/credentials`'s chart panels — a grid item with `overflow:hidden` collapsed to 42-158px instead of sizing to its real ~300px chart content, per a genuine, documented CSS Grid/Flexbox spec interaction. Fixed at the shared component CSS level with a scoped `:has(.dashboard-chart)` minimum height, protecting all 36 real pages using this shared chart-panel component rather than patching 2 pages individually. Being pure CSS, verified through the full dist-patch-and-restore pipeline with live `getComputedStyle` measurements and fresh screenshots confirming real bars now render.
- **(Cycle 206)** Chased down the visual-polish thread's scariest-looking finding yet (garbled overlapping patient-card text) and correctly identified it as a dist-staleness false positive rather than fixing something current source had already resolved — verified precisely via a source-vs-compiled-bundle comparison, not a guess. Found and fixed 2 real bugs instead: a CSS Grid default-value interaction inflating a 3-line status card to 711px on `/emergency/settings` (`align-content: start`, one line, verified via live dist-patch measurement), and a visible double-period typo on `/emergency/referrals` from concatenating an already-punctuated error message.
- **(Cycle 207)** Corrected a real accounting error in item #22's own running total (114→99 of 182 confirmed/likely covered, after finding ~12 route leaf-names had been silently double-audited across the pre-session/session boundary) before continuing the thread. Found and fixed a genuine WCAG contrast failure (1.41:1 → 6.47:1) on the ED's critical-alert "Acknowledge" button, caused by 3 independent CSS substring-match sweep rules all mis-firing on the same element — the identical footgun class Cycle 199 first found, now confirmed recurring in a second rule family. Also confirmed `/workspace`/`/start`'s ErrorBoundary crash is a stale-`dist/`-artifact false positive of an already-fixed Cycle 154 bug, not a new one.
- **(Cycle 208)** Closed out the `.profile-copilot-card__cta` contrast bug Cycle 207 flagged but didn't fix (1.41:1 → 6.60:1) — this time walked the live rule cascade before patching, found only 1 of the 2 usual sweep rules actually needed excluding (the other already resolved to the correct value for this component), and fixed precisely that one rather than reflexively copying the prior fix's shape. Attempted to also verify the 2 remaining unverified `__cta` candidates via this repo's own existing reception-role QA script, found the script's demo-auth payload is itself stale against the current user-hydration logic — left both honestly unfixed rather than guessing.
- **(Cycle 209)** Found and fixed 2 real bugs on `/live-map`: every "Tracked assets" card ran its title/meta/subtitle together with zero space (missing stacking layout on 3 inline children) — fixed with `display:flex; flex-direction:column`; and SVG marker labels rendered ~7x their own marker's diameter (never-set `font-size` defaulting to a browser default interpreted as raw SVG viewBox units) — fixed with an explicit `font-size`. Also found and fixed the data half of the same page's bug: a tautological ternary (`x ? y : y`) in `buildFleetTrackingMarkers` unconditionally duplicated the vehicle's status word, redundant with what the UI already showed in a sibling element.
- **(Cycle 210)** Found and fixed a real, previously-uncaught crash on `/legal/consent-history`: two call sites passed a plain `{label, onClick}` object as `EmptyState`'s `action` prop, which React can't render as a child ("Objects are not valid as a React child") — confirmed with a genuine `react-dom/server` reproduction proving the exact crash message, then the exact fix rendering clean. Also fixed a quieter sibling bug at the same call sites (`message` passed instead of the real `description` prop, silently dropping the error text). Added the component's first-ever test coverage (3 tests).
- **(Cycle 212)** Closed out the 17-cycle route-audit thread — all 179 of the app's distinct real routes now looked at at least once. Found and fixed a small real acronym-casing bug ("Api Health"/"Ai Health" → "API Health"/"AI Health") on `/saas-health`, the same bug shape Cycle 200 found (a blind case-transform over a small, fixed id set), fixed the same way (explicit correct labels, verified byte-for-byte via a standalone script).

Node's run-to-completion model makes a synchronous method atomic with respect to every other request, so no live race exists today. The one theoretical risk (multiple horizontally-scaled instances diverging, since reads never hit the DB) doesn't apply either — `docker-compose.app.yml` runs a single backend instance, no `replicas`/cluster config. Downgraded P1→P2: the real motivations for "Phase 2" are horizontal-scalability readiness and crash-durability, not an active bug. Added a class-level doc comment plus a new enforced regression test (`emergency-patient-atomicity-invariant.spec.ts`) so the current safety — accidental today, since nothing stops a future `await` from silently reintroducing a real race — becomes structural. Verified the guard works: temporarily made `movePatientToState` async with a real `await`, confirmed the test fails and names the method, reverted, confirmed green. Full backend suite 267/267 suites, 2208/2208 tests (1 first-run failure in `encryption.service.spec.ts` confirmed a pre-existing timing flake via isolated re-run, 37/37 clean — same class of flake as Cycle 222). |

Frontend unchanged (24 high, same chains). Backend's high count grew 34→38 — traced every newly-visible name (`typeorm`, `@nestjs/cli`, `@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin`/`parser`/`type-utils`/`typescript-estree`/`utils`, `create-jest`, `fork-ts-checker-webpack-plugin`) rather than assuming they were new advisories, and all resolve to the same already-known `brace-expansion` chain, reached via a 4th independent path this cycle found: `typeorm` (a direct, actively-used dependency, not dev-only tooling) bundles its own `glob`, itself pinning vulnerable `minimatch`/`brace-expansion`. `npm audit fix --dry-run` confirms `typeorm  0.0.2-alpha - 0.4.0-alpha.1` — no published version resolves clean — same "no safe path without an upstream fix" conclusion as the other 3 chains, just a wider blast radius now that a runtime (not build-only) dependency carries it too. Real-world exploitability assessment unchanged: DoS-class, requires attacker-controlled glob-pattern input, not reachable via this app's actual request surface. Originally traced to one shared advisory reached via 3 independent, each individually-confirmed-unfixable paths (Cycle 190): **(1)** `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y@6.10.2` (the actual latest release, confirmed via `npm view`) still declares `minimatch: ^3.1.2`; that line's newest release (`3.1.5`) requires `brace-expansion: ^1.1.7`; that line's newest release (`1.1.16`) is still inside the vulnerable range — no compatible patched release exists anywhere in the chain, on either tree. **(2)** `jest`'s internal `@jest/reporters`/`babel-plugin-istanbul` chain pins an old `glob@7.2.3`; `npm audit`'s suggested "fix" (`jest@25.0.0`) is actually a downgrade from the real installed version (`29.7.0` backend / `30.4.2` frontend) — confirmed by checking real installed versions rather than trusting the audit's version number at face value. **(3)** Backend only: `firebase-admin`'s `google-gax` and `mongoose`'s `gcp-metadata`→`gaxios` chains each pin their own internal `rimraf@5.0.10`→`glob@10.5.0`, invisible to and unfixable by any change to this repo's own `package.json`. All 3 need an upstream release from a third-party maintainer (`eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`, `jest`, or Google's `google-gax`/`gaxios`) that this program can't force. Real-world exploitability is low across all 3 — every affected package is dev-only build/test tooling or a GCP SDK's internal file-cleanup utility, never reached by untrusted runtime input in this application's actual attack surface. One small, verified, real (if audit-count-invisible) improvement made this cycle: backend's own direct `rimraf` bumped 5.0.5→6.1.3 (genuine forward upgrade, not a downgrade), cleaning this repo's own `prebuild` script dependency to a modern `glob@13.0.6` — does not reduce the audit's reported count since chains (2) and (3) above persist independently of this repo's direct dependencies. |

Fixed with `flex-shrink: 0; white-space: nowrap`. **Cycle 199** (highest blast radius): a large solid dark-navy rectangle with no content on every command-center-style page — the section wrapping the "Critical actions" cards (`.cdl-zone--primary-actions`) was being painted dark by an unrelated, deliberate app-wide `[class*='--primary']` button sweep rule that, as a plain substring match, also caught this layout zone's unrelated class name. Fixed with a targeted `:not()` exclusion. **Cycle 200 finding** (first content/copy bug, not layout/CSS): the Hospital Maturity Assessment page asks "How mature is your hospital's ai maturity?" / "...iot readiness?" — `hospitalReadinessAssessment.ts`'s question template blindly calls `.toLowerCase()` on the whole dimension label to make it read grammatically mid-sentence, which also destroys the "AI"/"IoT" acronyms in 2 of the 6 dimensions. Fixed by adding an explicit, already-correctly-cased `midSentenceLabel` field per dimension (data correct by construction, not a smarter-but-still-fragile detection heuristic) rather than transforming `label` (left untouched, since it's used elsewhere as a real heading). Being a JS/data change rather than CSS, this fix couldn't use the dist-CSS-patch verification technique (a JS bundle rebuild hits the same blocked Vite path) — verified instead via a standalone Node script computing all 6 question strings before/after, confirming byte-for-byte that only the 2 broken ones changed. **Cycle 203** (first backend-service bug in the thread, found via a same-condition side-by-side comparison across 2 pages rather than one page in isolation): `/notifications` leaked a raw `SyntaxError` ("Unexpected token '<'...") straight into the UI on a failed API call, while `/security`/`/audit` handled the identical "backend returned HTML" condition with a clean, designed message via the shared `apiClient.ts` `parseApiResponse` guard. `NotificationService.ts` had 4 methods calling raw `response.json()` instead of the file's own already-correct `parseApiResponse` pattern (used correctly by its other 4 sibling methods) — fixed all 4 to match. Also found the real service had zero real test coverage (the same-named test file tests an unrelated legacy fixture; the component test mocks the service wholesale) — added a new suite mocking only the network boundary, the correct level to catch this bug class. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint` (0 errors) plus a standalone Node script reconstructing the real `parseApiResponse` logic verbatim, proving the exact before/after strings byte-for-byte. **Cycle 204** (highest-severity finding in the thread — real functional content, not styling, silently missing): `/alerts` showed correct summary numbers but zero actual alert list below them. Traced to `ClinicalAlertsPage.tsx` passing its real content to the shared `EmergencyRoutePage` wrapper via a nonexistent `activeWork` prop — the component only ever read `children`, and since its props are typed `any`, the mismatch was silently swallowed rather than caught at compile time. Grepping every usage found the identical mistake independently made in `HospitalCommandCenter.tsx` too (the Command Center's entire "Sentinel EMS command" panel), confirming a real API footgun rather than a one-off typo. Fixed at the component level (`activeWork ?? children`), restoring both pages with zero changes to either page's own source. Also confirmed the dark-rectangle pattern recurring on `/dashboard` is *not* a new instance — same class Cycle 199 already excluded in source, just still visible in the stale, never-rebuilt `dist/` artifact this whole pipeline screenshots from. **Cycle 205**: `/competencies` and `/credentials` both showed a "status mix" bar chart panel that cut off immediately after its title, with no bars visible. Root-caused to a genuine CSS Grid + `overflow:hidden` interaction: a grid item with `overflow:hidden` gets an implied automatic minimum size of 0 rather than sizing to its real content, so `.dashboard-visual-panel` collapsed to 42px/158px despite containing a 210-240px chart — confirmed by comparing against a working chart panel that happened to sit inside an extra intermediate grid wrapper the 2 broken pages lack. Fixed at the shared component level (`DashboardVisualizations.css`, `.dashboard-visual-panel:has(.dashboard-chart) { min-height: 300px }`) rather than patching either page — this protects all 36 real pages using the shared `VisualizationPanel` component, not just the 2 confirmed broken. Being pure CSS, verified through the full dist-patch-and-restore pipeline: live `getComputedStyle` confirmed the height jump, and fresh screenshots confirmed both charts now render real bars with axis labels, matching every other working chart. **Cycle 206**: chased down what initially looked like the thread's worst finding yet — badly overlapping/garbled text on `/emergency/patients`'s patient cards — and correctly ruled it out as a dist-staleness false positive (current source has already restructured this exact area into a collapsed disclosure; the compiled `dist/` bundle just hasn't caught up), recording it as a confirmed non-issue rather than fixing something already fixed. Found and fixed 2 real bugs in the same batch instead: `/emergency/settings`'s risk indicator card was inflating 3 short text lines across a 711px box (a genuine CSS Grid default-value interaction — a narrow card stretched tall by taller siblings, then its own default `align-content:stretch` spread its rows to fill that height) — fixed with one line, `align-content: start`, verified via the full dist-patch-and-restore pipeline with live measurements and a fresh screenshot. `/emergency/referrals` showed a visible double-period typo from concatenating an already-punctuated error message with more literal text — fixed by stripping trailing periods before appending, verified via a standalone script across 4 real message shapes. All 12 findings verified appropriately to their kind via the `dist/` + static-serve + system-Edge pipeline established Cycle 196 (7 visually incl. 2 dist-patch, 1 mechanically, 3 via standalone logic reconstruction/component test, 1 confirmed-non-issue via source/compiled-bundle comparison). This is a real, ongoing category of work distinct from items #7 (Playwright/axe-core/Lighthouse, machine-checkable a11y/perf) and #9 (inline-style CI gate, a lint rule) — subjective design-quality judgment applied by actually rendering and looking at pages. **Cycle 207**: before continuing, corrected a real double-counting error in this row's own running total — reconstructing exactly which routes Cycles 196–206 covered found ~12 route leaf-names (`triage`, `alerts`, `laboratory`, `simulation`, `billing`, `notifications`, `security`, `audit`, `dashboard`, `staff`, `reports`, `timeline`) had been independently screenshotted in both an earlier cycle (198–200) and a later one (203–204) without either recognizing the repeat — the "114" figure was double-counting, not 114 distinct routes. Rebuilt the true covered set (56 exact-path-verified from Cycles 203–206's on-disk screenshot batches, plus 43 best-effort fuzzy-matched from Cycles 196–200's named pages): **99 of 182 confirmed/likely covered**, corrected the same way Cycle 153 corrected the "4 overlapping registries" claim. Also found the crawl's own 182-route inventory has 3 literal path duplicates (`/emergency/whiteboard`, `/emergency/intake`, `/emergency/queues`, each counted twice), so the true denominator is 179 distinct routes. With that honest baseline, screenshotted 14 confirmed-fresh routes (`/emergency/intake`, `/emergency/patient-room`, `/emergency/copilot`, `/emergency/provincial-health`, `/emergency/federated-learning`, `/emergency/digital-twin`, `/emergency/ai-governance`, `/emergency/tools`, `/emergency/ed-readiness`, `/emergency/diagnostics`, `/emergency/integrations`, `/workspace`, `/operations-center`, `/documentation` — running total **113 of 179**). `/workspace`/`/start` threw the app's ErrorBoundary — traced via source grep + `git log` + diff to `PlatformEntryHub`'s exact Cycle 154 crash, already fixed 2026-07-22 (`a5bd6e2f`) with its own regression test; the static `dist/` build (2026-07-19) simply predates the fix by 3 days — confirmed false positive, no action needed. Found and fixed a real WCAG contrast failure on the critical-alert "Acknowledge" button (`.fj-critical-banner__cta`, live on 4 routes: `ed-readiness`/`diagnostics`/`handoffs`/`reports`): measured 1.41:1 contrast (WCAG AA needs 4.5:1) caused by 3 independent CSS substring-match sweep rules (`color-normalization.css`'s `[class*='cta']`, plus 2 rules in `text-normalization.css`) all overriding this button's deliberate white-on-red styling because its class name doesn't follow the `--solid`/`--primary` BEM-modifier convention those rules assume — the same footgun class Cycle 199 found, now confirmed in a second rule family. Fixed with 3 targeted `:not(.fj-critical-banner__cta)` exclusions; verified live via dist-patch (1.41:1 → 6.47:1, screenshot-confirmed) plus a new 4-assertion regression test proven red/green via a standalone script. Also found `.profile-copilot-card__cta` live-measured with the identical broken colors, but its fix needs a separate design judgment call (different, non-critical background intent) — flagged, not fixed. **Cycle 208**: closed out that flagged follow-up instead of opening a new route batch. Re-derived `.profile-copilot-card__cta`'s intended design from its own CSS (`profile-surface-normalization.css`): a light muted surface with link-colored text, a secondary/ghost button, not a solid one — confirmed the same 1.41:1 contrast live. This time walked the *full* matching-rule cascade before patching rather than assuming the same 3-rule shape as the prior fix: since this is a real `<button>` (not an `<a>`), `text-normalization.css`'s plain-anchor rule never matches it at all, and its `__cta`-specific rule *does* match but already resolves to the same `--medical-text-link` token the component wants — so only `color-normalization.css`'s background sweep was actually wrong. Verified by patching only that one rule and re-measuring live before adding anything further: contrast rose to 6.60:1, confirmed by both the number and a fresh screenshot of a clean, readable button. Fixed with one `:not(.profile-copilot-card__cta)` exclusion (not three), avoiding an unnecessary, potentially-confusing edit to a rule that was already correct for this element. Also attempted to verify the 2 other `__cta` candidates Cycle 207 left unverified (`reception-job-profile-card__cta`, `reception-skill-strip__cta` — both gated behind a reception-facing demo role) using this repo's own existing QA tooling (`scripts/capture-reception-screenshots.mjs`'s `localStorage`-seeded demo-auth pattern) — the storage keys still match current config, but the role switch silently didn't take, traced to `UserContext.tsx`'s `hydrateStoredDemoUser()` apparently needing a richer payload shape than that script provides. Concluded the script itself has drifted stale (the same QA-infrastructure-rot pattern this program has found before — stale `.jsx`-only orphan-detection filters, a legacy test fixture) rather than reverse-engineering the correct shape mid-cycle; left both candidates honestly unfixed rather than guessing. **Cycle 209**: resumed the route batch, screenshotting 14 more (`/discover`, `/recommendations`, `/automation`, `/automation-audit`, `/automation-analytics`, `/assistant`, `/ai-command-center`, `/tools`, `/operations`, `/integrations/hub`, `/cosmos`, `/knowledge-graph`, `/3d-viewer`, `/live-map` — running total **127 of 179**). Two pages (`/ai-command-center`, `/cosmos`) fell back to the shared command-center dashboard and showed the exact `.cdl-zone--primary-actions` dark rectangle Cycle 199 already fixed; one (`/integrations/hub`) fell back to `/emergency/settings` and showed the exact pre-fix tall risk card Cycle 206 already fixed — both re-confirmed via source checks as still-present fixes in current CSS, just not yet reflected in the stale `dist/`; correctly not re-fixed. Found and fixed 2 real bugs on `/live-map`, plus a 3rd contributing cause to one of them: **(1)** the "Tracked assets" list's `<strong>`/`<span>`/`<small>` children had no stacking layout at all (all inline by default), so every card ran its 3 lines together with zero space ("North routeFLEET · occupiedoccupied · CareDroid North Clinic · ETA 18m") — fixed with `display:flex; flex-direction:column; gap:2px` on `.live-map__marker-item`, verified live via dist-patch that the 3 lines now render separately. **(2)** the map canvas's SVG "F"/"I" marker labels rendered at roughly 7x the diameter of their own marker dot — traced to an SVG `<text>` element that never had a `font-size` set anywhere, defaulting to the browser's ~16px text size interpreted directly as raw viewBox user-units next to a radius-2.2 circle; fixed with an explicit `font-size: 2.4px`, verified live via the same dist-patch pipeline showing small, legible glyphs. **(3)**, the word-level half of finding (1) — "occupiedoccupied" isn't just a missing space, the word is genuinely repeated — traced to a tautological ternary in `buildFleetTrackingMarkers` (`FLEET_STATUS_TONE[vehicle.status] ? vehicle.status : vehicle.status`, both branches identical) unconditionally duplicating the status already shown in a sibling UI element; fixed by deleting the dead ternary and the redundant subtitle field entirely (confirmed `FLEET_STATUS_TONE` remains meaningfully used elsewhere first, so nothing orphaned). Being a JS/TS logic change, verified via a standalone Node script reconstructing the real old/new logic verbatim against all 4 real vehicle statuses from the actual screenshot — old always duplicated, new never does — plus 2 new tests added to the existing `liveTrackingMapModel.test.ts`. **Cycle 210**: continued the batch, screenshotting 14 more (`/auth`, `/auth-callback`, `/reset-password`, `/verify-email`, `/legal/privacy-policy`, `/legal/terms`, `/legal/gdpr`, `/legal/hipaa`, `/legal/consent-history`, `/medical-iot`, `/fleet/command`, `/fleet/map`, `/digital-twin`, `/version` — running total **141 of 179**), finally covering this app's untouched auth/legal category. The 4 auth-prefixed routes all redirect cleanly to `/emergency/reception`, consistent with this app's documented no-login-UI design; `/digital-twin` deep-links into `/emergency/tools`, an intentional redirect; the rest render clean, real content. Found and fixed a genuine crash on `/legal/consent-history`: both its error-state and empty-state branches called the shared `EmptyState` component with `action={{label, onClick}}` — a plain object — but `EmptyState` renders `action` directly as `{action}`, and React throws "Objects are not valid as a React child" for a non-ReactNode value; confirmed with a real `react-dom/server` reproduction (not just read the code) that the exact pre-fix shape throws the exact real error, and the exact post-fix shape (a real `<button>` element) renders clean. Also fixed a quieter sibling bug at the same 2 call sites — `message` was passed instead of `EmptyState`'s real `description` prop, so the actual error text was silently dropped even before the crash. This component had zero prior test coverage; added 3 tests (`ConsentHistory.test.tsx`) covering the disabled-capability path, the fetch-failure path, and a real successful render. **Cycle 211**: continued the batch, screenshotting 14 more (`/auth/forgot-password`, `/auth/magic-link`, `/auth/invite`, `/surveillance/nexus`, `/digital-twin-intelligence`, `/customer-portal`, `/knowledge-base`, `/enterprise-readiness`, `/trackmind`, `/trackmind-maturity`, `/enterprise-platform`, `/platform-intelligence`, `/platform-admin`, `/workflow-mining` — running total **155 of 179**). Most fell back cleanly to shared pages, as expected. `/workflow-mining`'s "Journey frequency" chart showed clearly clipped diagonal axis labels ("ED triage calculator to dashboard" → "...ulator to dashboard") — real, reproducible, and traced to the shared `CategoryBarChart` component used by **42 real pages**, so investigated carefully before touching anything. Found a genuinely multi-layered root cause: the chart's own zero `margin.left` gives its rotated (`-20°`, `textAnchor="end"`) labels no room to swing left into; the shared `.dashboard-visual-panel` container (touched once before, Cycle 205) independently clips via `overflow: hidden`; and the app shell's own top-level scroll containers add a further boundary that's unsafe to touch at all. Tried 2 fix directions, verified each empirically: bumping the chart's internal `margin.left` (via the compiled-JS-literal-patch-and-restore technique) shifted the layout as expected but caused category bands to compress enough that adjacent rotated labels started overlapping each other at the values needed to meaningfully help — trading one clipping bug for a different one. Overriding the panel/chart container's `overflow: hidden` fixed 2 of 3 labels cleanly with no visible side effects, but the 3rd (longest-reaching) label still clipped against the untouchable app-shell boundary. Concluded a *safe*, fully-verified fix isn't achievable this cycle given this component's 42-consumer blast radius and the blocked JS-rebuild path's limits on testing across genuinely different label-length data — documented the full root-cause chain and both partial attempts for a dedicated future cycle rather than ship something half-understood, the same discipline as Cycle 208's honestly-unverified `__cta` candidates. No source files changed. **Cycle 212**: closed out the thread — screenshotted the final 24 previously-unaudited routes (`/start`, `/workspace-dependency-graph`, `/tools/catalog`, `/plugins`, `/dependency-map`, `/dependency-graph`, `/data-lineage`, `/self-diagnostics`, `/saas-health`, `/ai-governance`, `/regulatory`, `/artifacts`, `/memory`, `/fleet/predictive-maintenance`, `/fleet/route-optimizer`, `/ai-evaluation`, `/platform-learning-engine`, `/business-brain`, `/asset-packs`, `/expansion-opportunities`, `/integrations-marketplace`, `/integration-readiness`, `/solution-builder`, `/configuration-studio`) — **all 179 of the app's distinct real routes have now been screenshotted and looked at through this lens at least once, across 17 cycles (196-212)**. `/start` re-confirmed the same already-fixed `PlatformEntryHub`/Cycle 154 dist-staleness crash first found on `/workspace` in Cycle 207 — no new investigation, already thoroughly documented. `/dependency-graph` showed another live instance of Cycle 211's already-investigated-and-deliberately-unfixed `CategoryBarChart` label-clipping bug (same shared component, same failure shape) — recorded as a further confirmed instance, not re-investigated. Found and fixed a real, small acronym-casing bug on `/saas-health`: health-check cards read "Frontend Health," "Backend Health"... but "**Api** Health" and "**Ai** Health," missing their correct acronym capitalization. Traced to `saasHealthApi.ts`'s `SAAS_HEALTH_FALLBACK` constant (rendered whenever the real backend is unreachable — unconditional in this static-serve environment, and a real production scenario too), which derived labels via a blind `${id.charAt(0).toUpperCase()}${id.slice(1)} Health` transform over a small, fixed set of 7 ids — correct for 5 ordinary words, wrong for the 2 that are themselves acronyms. The exact same bug *shape* Cycle 200 found (a blind case transform blind to acronyms), fixed the same way: explicit, correctly-cased labels per id rather than a smarter detection heuristic, verified byte-for-byte via a standalone script (confirmed only `api`/`ai` changed, the other 5 identical). Added the component's first-ever test coverage (3 tests). **Route-audit thread now formally closed** — future cycles on this item should look for genuinely new visual-polish issues on already-seen pages, or re-audit as source continues to change, rather than treating "still need to find an unaudited route" as the driving question. **Cycle 213**: closed the 2 candidates left flagged since Cycle 207 (`reception-job-profile-card__cta` on `/profile`, `reception-skill-strip__cta` on `/emergency/reception`). Root-caused Cycle 208's "role switch silently didn't take" observation precisely: `scripts/capture-reception-screenshots.mjs`'s seeded demo profile had no `authMode`/`demoPersona` marker, so `demoPersonaModel.ts`'s `isDemoPersonaUser()` never recognized it, and `hydrateStoredDemoUser()` silently discarded the entire payload — including the requested role — falling back to the app's default role every time. Fixed the script (`authMode: 'open-access'`). But found this alone still couldn't render either CTA: both components were added 2026-07-24, 5 days after `dist/`'s 2026-07-19 build, so the served bundle contains none of this code at all — a harder blocker than ordinary dist staleness (which affects whether a *fix* shows, not whether a *feature* exists). Verified instead with a new technique: a hand-built HTML harness loading the real current source CSS directly via native `@import`/`<link>` (no bundler, sidesteps the blocked Vite/esbuild path) against markup matching each component's real JSX, run through the same Playwright+system-Edge pipeline. Measured **~1.02:1** contrast — the worst instance of this sweep-rule bug found yet — confirmed by the same 2-3 CSS files as Cycles 207-208 not recognizing either class as an already-solid button. Fixed with the same `:not()` exclusion pattern (re-measured live: 4.10:1 — still short of AA). That uncovered a second, genuinely separate pre-existing defect once the masking sweep was excluded: both components' own `--cdl-brand-600` background token itself only reaches 4.10:1 (light) / 2.77:1 (dark) with white text — neither passes AA, and no prior cycle had ever seen the component's *true* unmasked colors to notice. Fixed by switching to a fixed, theme-constant `#075985` (7.56:1 in both themes, verified live) instead of a theme-swapping token. Added 5 new regression assertions, `tsc`/`eslint` both 0 errors. |
| 23 | P2 OPEN (Cycle 201: 1st real bug found+fixed; Cycle 202: blast-radius check, none found) | Real full-stack (live backend + live frontend, unmocked) integration verification — a new technique this program had never applied to the AI chat feature before | Opened per a direct request to verify the AI is actually interactive, not just rendering correctly. **Cycle 201**: drove real compiled backend (`nest build` + `node dist/backend/src/main.js`, served together with the frontend's static `dist/` via `NODE_ENV=production`'s `registerProductionFrontendAssets`) + a real Playwright/system-Edge browser through an actual `POST /api/auth/dev-session` bootstrap and a real Copilot chat send — something no unit test (mocks HTTP), hermetic e2e spec (stubs the network before any request reaches a server), or static-analysis cycle had ever done. Found **every real call to `POST /api/ai/node/conversational` failed with 400 "property stream should not exist,"** unconditionally, since 2026-06-13 (`git log -S`, commit `9434922c`) — the browser client always sends a `stream` key the backend's whitelist-validated DTO never declared. Fixed by adding the field to `UnifiedNodeConversationalDto`; wrote a genuine `class-validator`-based regression test (confirmed red without the fix, green with it) since the DTO's only prior spec was a source-text `toContain` check that could never have caught this. Also confirmed, with real evidence (not assumption), that the entitlement 403s encountered immediately after (`PROFESSIONAL` tier + `pack-required` for `agent-clinical`) are deliberate, documented SaaS monetization gates (`entitlements.config.ts`), not bugs — correctly left untouched. Also confirmed a raw self-registration path hits a separate, expected dead end (no org auto-provisioned) since this app has no exposed login UI post-"auth UI removal" — the real supported local/demo entry point is `dev-session`, which is what surfaced the actual bug. Also re-ran the pre-existing, real `e2e/interactive-ai.spec.mjs` suite (IX12-14) for the first time since 2026-07-18 (predates the Cycle 160 `esbuild` block) — 8/8 clean, confirming the streaming-phases/keyboard-operable/collaboration/command-palette interaction layer is solid in the mode it's designed to run in. This blocked the platform's single most visible AI-interactive feature, completely, for anyone who ever reached a real backend. **Cycle 202**: checked whether the same bug class (a hand-duplicated backend DTO silently drifting from its own frontend caller's request shape) recurs on the app's other "canonical" AI entry point, `POST /api/ai/node` (structured/intent-based) — confirmed clean, both statically (its DTO and the frontend's `CareDroidAIRequest` interface declare the identical 3 fields, no independent duplication) and via a real live request through the real backend (validation passed, reached the same already-known-legitimate entitlement gate, no new 400). Root cause is now precisely scoped: the risk is specifically hand-duplicated request/DTO shapes with no compile-time link across the frontend/backend boundary, not strict validation itself (which is working correctly everywhere checked). Auditing all ~146 real POST/PATCH/PUT endpoints for the same risk is out of scope for one cycle — downgraded to P2 (the acute, platform-wide-blocking instance is fixed; what remains is a lower-urgency pattern to stay alert to, not an active break) rather than claimed as exhaustively swept. **Cycle 234**: ran, for the first time, a real live-backend HTTP round trip (not source reading, not mocked-repository unit tests) against both `/emergency/patients` and `/emergency/intake/vertical-slice` using payload shapes reconstructed from the actual current `QuickIntake.tsx`/`smartIntakeVerticalSlice.ts` source. Cycles 192/193/223's flags/vitals/extra-field normalization all confirmed correct under a genuine SQLite persistence round trip. See item #23 for the one new finding (timeline-metadata discard) this surfaced. The DTO-building/whitelist-enforcement work itself is still open — this cycle only extended verification depth, no schema was built. |
| 24 | ~~P2~~ DONE (Cycle 217; hardened Cycle 218; loose end closed Cycle 219) | Real bug: the literal `admin` emergency role loses every command-palette entry with a `requiredAction` | **Closed 2026-07-28 (Cycle 217), hardened further 2026-07-28 (Cycle 218), last flagged loose end closed 2026-07-28 (Cycle 219).** **Cycle 219**: the "not chased" note below (`demoPersonaModel.ts` silently falling back to `physician` for an unrecognized seeded role) was traced to its real source — `normalizeEmergencyRole()`'s own fallback, previously `EMERGENCY_ROLE_IDS.physician` for *any* unrecognized string, not just this one demo-persona path. Fixed to fail closed to `EMERGENCY_ROLE_IDS.readOnlyViewer` instead, matching `canonicalAccess.ts`'s own already-safe `demo_observer` fallback for the identical situation (same underlying `emergencyRoleId`, `'read_only_viewer'`, on both sides now). See item log below for the full writeup. **Cycle 218**: systematically audited the whole alias table for the same bug *shape* (70 checks: every catalog role's self-key + declared aliases) and found one more real instance — `demo_observer`'s own declared `'read-only-viewer'` alias was silently shadowed by `lab_technician`'s auto-derived `emergencyRoleId`-based alias (catalog order, not a seed this time; string currently unreachable from any real caller, unlike the `admin` case, so lower urgency but still a real declared-but-broken alias). Rather than patch the one string, restructured the alias table's construction into two explicit priority passes — every role's own self-key/declared `aliases` register first, auto-derived `emergencyRoleId`/`saasRole` fallbacks only fill remaining gaps — fixing this case and foreclosing the entire bug class for any future catalog addition, verified via a full 80-input before/after snapshot diff showing exactly the 2 intended lines changed and nothing else. Cycle 216 found this live via a side-by-side role comparison (under `role: 'admin'` every `requiredAction`-gated command — AI Assist, "Create patient," "Create referral," "Open reassessment" — vanished from the command palette; `charge_nurse` was unaffected) and traced it partway before running out of standalone-execution technique. Cycle 217 unlocked one: Node 24's `--experimental-strip-types`/`--experimental-transform-types` plus a small custom module-resolution loader (registered via `--experimental-loader`, not `--import` — the fix for last cycle's failed attempt) let real, unmodified `.ts` source run standalone. Used it to build the *actual* `compiledProfile` object the app builds at runtime and found the real bug: `getCanonicalRoleMapping('admin')` resolves to the `it_admin` catalog entry, not `super_admin` — one line in `canonicalAccess.ts`'s alias-seed table, `admin: 'it_admin'`, silently shadowing `super_admin`'s own explicit `aliases: ['admin', ...]` declaration (the seed's `if (!acc[key])` guard sets once, permanently, regardless of catalog order). `git blame` showed both lines landed in the same commit (`bb18f2b7`, 2026-06-28) — an internal inconsistency, not intentional; `it_admin`'s own alias list never includes `'admin'`. Fixed by removing the shadowing seed entry. Verified precisely via the same real-source trace (before/after: `hospitalRole` `it_admin`→`super_admin`, final role `it_admin`→`admin`, `presentEmergencyPermission` `hidden`→`allowed`, `routeAccess` 45→48 routes) and confirmed zero regressions across all 12 of `canonicalAccess.test.ts`'s existing alias-resolution cases; corrected the one test that had itself encoded the bug. Confirmed live production impact, not just a QA-fixture artifact: `emergency-os-onboarding.defaults.ts` maps real hospital-admin onboarding labels (`clinic-administrator`, `hospital-administrator`) to this same `admin` role — real onboarded hospital administrators have been silently restricted to IT-admin-only access since 2026-06-28. Cycle 216's `charge_nurse` test-seed workaround in `interactive-ai.spec.mjs` was intentionally left in place rather than reverted to `admin`, since `dist/` remains stale and can't verify the fix live without a blocked rebuild — safe to revert in a future cycle once a real rebuild is possible. `demoPersonaModel.ts`'s silent fallback-to-`physician` for an unrecognized seeded role string (e.g. `super_admin`) — noted as open/not-chased here at the time — was resolved at its actual source in Cycle 219 (see above). |
| 25 | ~~P2~~ DONE (Cycle 220) | Real bug: `normalizeSaasRole()` is case-sensitive, silently defaults a mis-cased role to `student` | Found while extending Cycles 217–219's frontend role-system-audit methodology to the backend for the first time. `saas-profile.constants.ts`'s `normalizeSaasRole()` compares a raw input string against ~15 lowercase literals with strict `===`, no `.trim().toLowerCase()` first — `'ADMIN'`/`'Admin'`/padded whitespace all silently fall through to the safe-but-wrong `student` (minimal-privilege) default instead of matching their real alias. Fails safe, not a security hole (unlike Cycle 219's `physician`-fallback fix), but genuinely client-reachable: `user-profile.service.ts` persists `dto.role` (a real API request body field) straight through this function into the stored `roleProfileId`. One candidate needed direct verification rather than trusting the sweep's read: `auth.service.ts`'s `validateOAuthLogin` — confirmed directly (not just via the sweep) that its `profile` parameter is typed `{id, email, name}` only, so there's no institution/specialty/licenseNumber data available to drop in the first place, not an exploitable gap. A second candidate, `ReferralService.createReferral` (backend, drops `service`/`respondedAt`/`completedAt`/`responseNote`/`updatedAt`/`summary` from the `Referral` type), turned out to have no live caller at all — `ReferralPanel.tsx`'s real referral-creation path is `emergencyStore.ts`'s local Zustand `createReferral` action, which never calls this backend endpoint and already preserves every field correctly on its own. Left unfixed per this program's "confirm real reachability before treating a type mismatch as more than theoretical" discipline (established Cycle 217) — no evidence of a live consequence. **Cycle 234**: extended this item's technique to patient creation (item #18's endpoints) for the first time — `nest build` + a live `node dist/backend/src/main.js` + a real `/api/auth/dev-session` bootstrap + real `fetch` POSTs, no mocks, real SQLite persistence. Reconstructed the real current wire shapes both `QuickIntake.tsx` and `SmartIntake.tsx`'s vertical-slice builder (`smartIntakeVerticalSlice.ts`) send today (re-verified against source, not assumed from Cycle 191's 43-cycles-old snapshot) and POSTed them at `/emergency/patients` and `/emergency/intake/vertical-slice`. Confirmed clean: Cycles 192/193/223's flag-object-to-string, bpSystolic/bpDiastolic-to-sbp/dbp, and extra-field-preservation fixes all hold correctly under real persistence; the controller's `dto.patient \|\| dto` dual-shape handling (`emergency-os.controller.ts`'s `createSmartIntakeVerticalSlice`) correctly supports both the wrapped shape `SmartIntake.tsx` actually sends and the unwrapped shape used elsewhere — verified as intentional, not a bug. Found one real, live-confirmed gap along the way: `SmartIntakeService.createVerticalSlice()` unconditionally overwrites whatever `timeline` the caller sends with its own 2 bare synthetic events (`emergency-os.services.ts`, `createPatient({ ...input, timeline: [arrivalEvent] })` followed by a `movePatientToState` append) — so the frontend's real AI-suggestion provenance (`confidence`, `ruleTriggered`, `suggestionReason`, `override` vs. `suggestedPriority`) is silently discarded on every real create-and-triage action, confirmed via the live response body (sent 3 metadata-bearing events, got back 2 bare ones). Grepped every real reader of backend-sourced timeline events and found none currently consume that metadata — `emergencyStore.ts`'s only `ruleTriggered`/`suggestionReason` references are a hardcoded demo fixture, not a live reader — so per the Cycle 194/217 "confirm real reachability before treating as more than theoretical" discipline, documented rather than fixed; a live consequence would first require some UI to actually read triage-suggestion provenance back off the patient record, which none does today. |

With no config source registered, `ConfigService.get('encryption')` returns `undefined`, so `EncryptionService` silently fell through to its hardcoded **insecure dev-fallback key** (`'0'.repeat(64)`, guarded by an `isDevelopment` check that defaults `nodeEnv` to `'development'` when unset) instead of the test's own `ENCRYPTION_MASTER_KEY` — two different keys, so cross-key re-encryption failed its GCM auth-tag check exactly as it should. **Confirmed production is unaffected** (`app.module.ts` correctly wires `ConfigModule.forRoot({isGlobal:true})` with the encryption config loaded) — this was purely a test-isolation gap. ## Methodology

Scores are this review's holistic judgment from cited evidence, not an automated formula. For the full cycle-by-cycle history (157+ cycles), see project memory `project-quality-baseline-cycle0.md`.

Every cycle entry — going forward, logged below as its own dated section rather than prepended into one growing line — should follow the same reusable three-bucket pattern:

- **Re-verified from scratch this pass** — things actually re-run this cycle, each with a fresh count.
- **Carried forward, not re-verified this pass** — things asserted true but not re-checked this cycle.
- **Not evaluated** — things out of scope / environment-blocked, stated honestly rather than silently assumed.

**Recurring verification toolkit:** `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + backend, full scope incl. tests), `eslint` (frontend + backend), `npm audit` (both trees), `npm run check:inline-styles` (the repo's own inline-style CI gate), a repo-wide ARIA bare-boolean/expression violation scan, the full Jest backend suite (`npm test` in `backend/`) and Vitest frontend suite (`npm run test:run`) with exact pass counts each time, a production build check (chunk size/gzip), real compiled-boot smoke tests against a live JWT hitting real endpoints, `madge --circular` (dependency-cycle scans), `ts-prune` (unused-export inventory), a Playwright/axe-core a11y suite routed through the system's installed Edge binary (working around a sandboxed Chromium block), a native-`PerformanceObserver`-based performance suite (`e2e/performance.spec.mjs`), the repo's various `scripts/audit-*.mjs` tools built up cycle over cycle (routes/nav, full-app crawl, backend-frontend exposure, etc. — see project memory for the full list), and — new as of Cycle 217 — standalone execution of real, unmodified `.ts` source modules via `node --experimental-strip-types --experimental-transform-types --experimental-loader=<custom-loader.mjs>` (a small resolver hook that appends missing extensions and handles JSON imports; must use `--experimental-loader`, not `--import`, which silently fails to register hook exports), for tracing pure-logic bugs (permission/role resolution, data transforms) precisely without needing the blocked Vite/esbuild bundler — works for `.ts` files without JSX/React dependencies; `.tsx` or React-importing chains still fail (no JSX transform available this way). Extended Cycle 220 to backend `.ts` modules too: TypeORM entity files' `@Entity()`/`@Column()` decorators aren't type annotations (type-stripping can't erase them), so a decorated file that's only needed for one plain, non-decorated enum export can be worked around with a loader-level import redirect to a small mock exposing just that enum, with its values copied verbatim from the real source and verified by `Read`, not guessed.

**File-structure rule going forward (the reason this file was rebuilt):** never prepend a new cycle's narrative into an existing line. Each cycle gets its own `###` heading under "Cycle log" below. When this section grows unwieldy again, rotate older entries into `docs/archive/` exactly as done here — don't let it regrow into a single-line file.

## Cycle log

### Cycle 158 (2026-07-23) — rebuilt this file from scratch; it had become structurally unreadable

The "Last updated" line at the top of the old `SCORECARD.md` had grown, by prepending each cycle's summary for 157 cycles, to 162,783 characters on one line — unreadable by the Read tool, and almost certainly by any other tooling or editor that assumes reasonable line lengths. A second section ("This pass's headline change") had the same accumulation shape. Rather than keep patching a file with this structural defect, archived the full prior version verbatim to `docs/archive/SCORECARD-archive-2026-07-23-cycles-1-157.md` (nothing lost — git history and project memory both also retain the full record) and rebuilt this file with real section headers instead of run-on lines.

While extracting the old file's content, found it was internally stale in ways not previously flagged: the "This pass's headline change" narrative log stopped at Cycle 106 (2026-07-18) even though the Category detail and Roadmap sections referenced work up to the 140s; the "Methodology" dated log stopped at Cycle 61 (2026-07-15). Neither gap is backfilled here — noted honestly in the roadmap above as a known limitation of the archive, not silently smoothed over.

Rather than just reformat, re-ran the checks that are cheap to re-run today rather than copy stale numbers forward unverified: `tsc --noEmit` (frontend + backend, both 0 errors), `eslint` (frontend, 0 errors), `npm run check:inline-styles` (110/690, improved from the last-recorded 137/690), and `npm audit` on both trees. The audits surfaced a real, previously unflagged regression: frontend dependency vulnerabilities went from a recorded 0 to 2 (1 high), and backend went from a recorded "4 known, unchanged" to 8, including **1 critical** — a `protobufjs` remote-code-execution family of advisories reachable via the already-tracked `@xenova/transformers` migration item, now meaningfully more urgent than its prior WARN framing suggested. Also kicked off full fresh runs of both the frontend Vitest suite and backend Jest suite for an honest, dated pass/fail count (see the Testing gauge above and cycle addendum below) rather than carrying forward the last-recorded 1688/1688 and 11547/11556 figures unverified.

Did not attempt to fix the dependency-audit regressions in this cycle — `npm audit fix --force` on the backend is a breaking downgrade of `@xenova/transformers` and deserves its own verified pass, not a drive-by inside a file-restructuring cycle. Logged as a top roadmap item instead.

Also ran both full test suites fresh rather than trust the last-recorded counts. Backend: 244/245 suites, 1917/1922 tests — 1 file failing (`test/tool-calling.spec.ts`, a stale test double missing `AiGatewayService.attachUnifiedNode`, a method that genuinely exists on the real class). ### Cycle 159 (2026-07-23) — root-caused and fixed all 18 test failures Cycle 158 found, plus 6 fresh tsc errors it missed

Picked up the top item Cycle 158 left open: 17 frontend files (25 tests) + 1 backend file, all genuinely failing. Rather than treat it as one blob, re-ran just the 17 failing files in isolation for full assertion-diff detail, then grouped the 25 failures into distinct root causes before touching anything — a mix of real refactors the tests hadn't caught up to, a few stale hardcoded values, and one real backend wiring gap:

Updated the 4 test files to check the real two-hop path.

Added all 21 entries after verifying each against the actual controller source (`@Get`/`@Post` decorators), not just trusting the test's diff.
- **Renamed file (`buildConfigConsistency`).** Test still pointed at `backend/src/config/database.config.ts`; real file is `database-url.config.ts` (same `buildPostgresOptions` export). One-line path fix.
- **Intentional soft-fail behavior (`smartIntakeApi`).** The concurrent session's `70cef8c5` deliberately changed `SmartIntakeApi.createSession` to soft-fail to a local demo session when offline instead of rejecting ("reception identity check must not hard-fail offline" — real UX intent, confirmed in the source comment). Test still expected the old hard-rejection. Updated to assert the new, correct behavior.
